Equation

J.3
in
Lemma

J.4
with
\textit{Q}_{0}=\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}
,
\Pi_{0}=\Pi_{n}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})
and
\upsilon=\upsilon_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\textup{regular}})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})]\leq\frac{1}{\upsilon_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\textup{KL}(\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n},\Pi_{n}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))]+\frac{1}{\upsilon_{n}}\textup{e}^{\upsilon_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\Pi_{n}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\textup{regular}}|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})],

where the first term goes to zero by
Theorem

3.3
. Thus, it remains to show that the second term goes to zero. Note that for any measurable function
\varphi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbbm{Y}_{n}\mapsto[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\textup{regular}}|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}\bigr)}\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})\right]}\leq\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})]+\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\textup{regular}}|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}\bigr)}(1-\varphi)\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})\right]}.

Let
\varphi_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\textup{ivB,regular}}(A_{n})}\varphi_{n,m}
. Then by definition,
A_{n}\epsilon_{n}\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}\geq\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger}\geq\zeta_{n,m}
for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\textup{ivB,regular}}(A_{n})
. Hence by
Item

A1
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n}\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})]\leq\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\textup{ivB,regular}}(A_{n})}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n,m}]

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{M}_{n}|\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n((A_{n}\epsilon_{n})\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{3}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n((A_{n}\epsilon_{n})\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})^{2}}.

Moreover, by
Item

A1
again,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\textup{regular}}}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{(n)}[1-\varphi_{n}]

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n((A_{n}\epsilon_{n})\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})^{2}}.

Using a similar argument to that in
J.12
in the proof of
Theorem

J.8
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{\textup{regular}}|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}\bigr)}(1-\varphi_{n})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n})\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}-\log\mathinner{(\alpha_{n,m_{n}^{*}})}+\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}^{*},\Pi_{n,m_{n}^{*}})-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n((A_{n}\epsilon_{n})\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{(A_{n}+\mathfrak{c}_{6}+\mathfrak{c}_{2}(1+\mathfrak{c}_{5})^{2})n\epsilon_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n((A_{n}\epsilon_{n})\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}n((A_{n}\epsilon_{n})\vee\zeta_{n}^{\ddagger})^{2}}

for some absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}>0
, which completes the proof.

J.5
Proofs for
Appendix

B

J.5.1
Proof of
Lemma

B.1

We divide the proof into two steps. The first step is to show that when the two conditions
B.1
and
B.2
are met, there is a good test for a convex alternative.

Lemma J.15
(Testing, convex alternatives).

Suppose that
B.1
and
B.2
holds. Then there exist absolute constants
\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}>0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that for any sufficiently large
n\in\mathbbm{N}
and any
\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1}\in\Lambda_{n}
, there exist a test function
\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbbm{Y}_{n}\mapsto[0,1]
satisfying

\max\mathinner{\left\{\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}}[\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}],\sup_{\bm{\lambda}\in\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda_{n},\mathscr{d}_{n}}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\zeta)}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}[1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}]\right\}}\leq 2\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}n\zeta^{2}},

(J.14)

whenever
\zeta<\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})
, where
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda_{n},\mathscr{d}_{n}}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\zeta)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}_{1})\leq\zeta\right\}}.

Proof J.16
.

Let
\zeta>0
be such that
\zeta<\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})
, where
\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}>0
will be specified later. For notational convenience, we denote the likelihood ratio function by

\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime};\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}

for
\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\Lambda
. We will show that the test function defined by

\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}(\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbbm{1}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})\leq 1\right)}

satisfies the desired error bound. For the type I error, by using Markov’s inequality and
B.1
, we get

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}}[\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}}\mathinner{\left((\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1}))^{\rho_{\circ}-1}\geq 1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left((\rho_{\circ}-1)\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\circ}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-(1-\rho_{\circ})\mathfrak{c}_{1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\Bigl(-\frac{(1-\rho_{\circ})\mathfrak{c}_{1}}{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}n\zeta^{2}\Bigr)}.

For the type II error, we consider the event

\mathbbm{B}_{n,\bm{\lambda}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}\in\mathbbm{Y}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\log(\sr_n(\blambda, \blambda_1))<\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta^{2}\right\}}

for each
\bm{\lambda}\in\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda_{n},\mathscr{d}_{n}}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\zeta)
, where
\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}>0
will be specified later. Then, since
\rho_{\blacklozenge}>1
and
\mathscr{d}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}_{1})\leq\zeta
, by Markov’s inequality and
B.2
.

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathbbm{B}_{n,\bm{\lambda}}^{\complement}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathinner{\left((\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}_{1}))^{\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1}>\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)n\zeta^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)n\zeta^{2}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathinner{\left[(\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}_{1}))^{\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1}\right]}

\displaystyle=\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)n\zeta^{2}}\exp\mathinner{\bigl((\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\blacklozenge}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)n\zeta^{2}+(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\bigl(-\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}-\mathfrak{c}_{2}\right)}(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)n\zeta^{2}\bigr)}.

By the definition of the set
\mathbbm{B}_{n,\bm{\lambda}}
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathinner{\left[(1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{B}_{n,\bm{\lambda}})\right]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}}\mathinner{\left[(1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n})\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}_{1})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{B}_{\bm{\lambda}})\right]}\leq\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta^{2}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}}\mathinner{\bigl[1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}\bigr]},

where, by using Markov’s inequality and
B.1
again, the expectation is further bounded as

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}}\mathinner{\left[1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}\right]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}}\mathinner{\left((\textsf{{r}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\bm{\lambda}_{0}))^{\rho_{\circ}-1}\geq 1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left((\rho_{\circ}-1)\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\circ}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-(1-\rho_{\circ})\mathfrak{c}_{1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\Bigl(-\frac{(1-\rho_{\circ})\mathfrak{c}_{1}}{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}n\zeta^{2}\Bigr)}.

Hence if we choose
\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}=2\mathfrak{c}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\mathfrak{c}_{1}(1-\rho_{\circ})/(4\mathfrak{c}_{2})\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\max

\displaystyle\mathinner{\left\{\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}}[\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}],\sup_{\bm{\lambda}\in\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda_{n},\mathscr{d}_{n}}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\zeta)}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}[1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}]\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\max\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{-2\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta^{2}},\sup_{\bm{\lambda}\in\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda_{n},\mathscr{d}_{n}}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\zeta)}\mathinner{\left\{\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathinner{\left[(1-\widetilde{\varphi}_{n})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{B}_{\bm{\lambda}})\right]}+\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}}(\mathbbm{B}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{\complement})\right\}}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\textup{e}^{-((\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)\wedge 2)\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\zeta^{2}},

which completes the proof.

Using the above lemma, we can prove
Lemma

B.1
under the additional assumption
B.3
.

Proof J.17
(Proof of
Lemma

B.1
).

Fix
\bm{\lambda}^{\star}\in\Lambda_{n}^{\star}
and
\zeta>\zeta_{n,m}
.
Define

\Theta_{n,m,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}j\zeta<\mathscr{d}_{n}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\leq(j+1)\zeta\right\}}.

For a small
u>0
which will be specified later, there exists a subset
\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\lambda}_{n,m,j,\ell}\right\}}_{\ell\in[N_{n,m,j}]}\subset\textsf{{T}}(\Theta_{n,m,j})
with
N_{n,m,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathscr{N}(uj\zeta,\Theta_{n,m,j},\mathscr{d}_{n})
such that

\Theta_{n,m,j}\subset\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{N_{n,m,j}}\Theta_{n,m,j,\ell}\mbox{ where }\Theta_{n,m,j,\ell}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}_{n,m,j,\ell})\leq uj\zeta\right\}}.

Then by
Lemma

J.15
, there exist absolute constants
\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}>0
, and
\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for any
n\in\mathbbm{N}
,
j>J_{0}
and
\ell\in[N_{n,m,j}]
, we have a test function
\varphi_{n,m,j,\ell}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbbm{Y}_{n}\mapsto[0,1]
that satisfies

\max\mathinner{\left\{\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}[\varphi_{n,m,j,\ell}],\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,m,j,\ell}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta})}[1-\varphi_{n,m,j,\ell}]\right\}}\leq 2\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}nj^{2}\zeta^{2}},

whenever
u<\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
, because
\mathscr{d}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\bm{\lambda}_{n,m,j,\ell})\geq j\zeta
. Then by
B.3
with a suitably chosen
u
, e.g.,
u=\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}/2
, and the assumption
\zeta>\zeta_{n,m}
, there exists an absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

N_{n,m,j}\leq\mathscr{N}(u(j\epsilon),\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\leq 2j\epsilon\right\}},\mathscr{d}_{n})\leq\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta_{n,m}^{2}}.

We now define the combined test function
\varphi_{n,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sup_{j\in\mathbbm{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}j>J_{0}}\sup_{\ell\in[N_{n,m,j}]}\varphi_{n,m,j,\ell}
. Then the type-I error of the test
\varphi_{n}
is bounded as

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n,m}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbbm{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}j>J_{0}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N_{n,m,j}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}[\varphi_{n,m,j,\ell}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbbm{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}j>J_{0}}N_{n,m,j}\mathinner{\left(2\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}nj^{2}\zeta^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{1-\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta^{2}}}\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta_{n,m}^{2}}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}nJ_{0}^{2}\zeta^{2}}

which can be further bounded by
\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{4}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta^{2}}
up to a constant factor for some
\mathfrak{c}_{4}^{\prime\prime}>0
if
J_{0}>\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}/\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
. The type II error is bounded as

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,m}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\geq\epsilon}\textsf{{P}}_{\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta})}^{(n)}[1-\varphi_{n,m}]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\in\mathbbm{N}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}j>J_{0}}\sup_{\ell\in[N_{n,m,j}]}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,m,j,\ell}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta})}[1-\varphi_{n,m,j,\ell}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime\prime}J_{0}^{2}n\zeta^{2}}.

Since
n\zeta^{2}\geq 1
by assumption, the constant multiplicative factors
2/(1-\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime\prime}n\zeta^{2}})
and
2
of the testing error bounds can be absorbed into the exponents when
J_{0}
is large. The proof is done.

J.5.2
Proof of
Lemma

B.3

Proof J.18
.

By the second assumption of the lemma, if
\rho-1\in[-\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime}]
, we have that

\displaystyle\textup{e}^{(\rho-1)\textsf{{D}}_{\rho}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}^{\rho-1}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}}^{(n)}\textup{e}^{(\rho-1)\mathinner{\left(\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{(\rho-1)\textup{KL}\mathinner{(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})}+\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}(\rho-1)^{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})}.

From the preceding display and the assumption that
(\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})\leq\textup{KL}\mathinner{(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})}\leq\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
, for
\rho_{\blacklozenge}>1
such that
\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1<\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\blacklozenge}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})\leq\mathinner{\left\{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)\right\}}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1})

and for
\rho_{\circ}\in(0,1)
such that
\rho_{\circ}>(1-\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime})\vee 0
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\circ}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}_{1}})\geq\mathinner{\left\{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}}+\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}(1-\rho_{\circ})\right\}}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda}_{0},\bm{\lambda}_{1}).

This completes the proof.

Appendix K
Proofs for deep learning applications in
Section

4
and
Appendix

D

Throughout this section, for a set of some network parameters
\tilde{\Theta}\subset\Theta
, we let
\textup{{net}}(\tilde{\Theta})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\bm{\theta}\in\tilde{\Theta}\right\}}
, which is a set of neural networks induced by network parameters in
\tilde{\Theta}
.

K.1
Preliminaries

K.1.1
Function approximation by neural networks

In this subsection, we give results on the approximation ability of neural networks for some smooth function classes. These are important for the proofs of
Corollaries

4.3
and
D.1
.

We first state the lemma on neural network approximation of Hölder smooth functions.

Theorem K.1
(Approximation of Hölder smooth functions).

Let
\beta>0
,
d\in\mathbbm{N}
, and
F_{0}>0
. Then there exist absolute constants
M_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
,
K_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}_{1}>0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{2}>0
such that for any
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq K_{0}}
,
M\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq M_{0}}
,
B>\mathfrak{c}_{1}M^{\max\{1,2(\beta-1)/d\}}
and
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}
, there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta^{\leq B}_{(K,M)}
such that

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-f^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\infty}\leq\mathfrak{c}_{2}\mathinner{\left(2^{-K}+M^{-2\beta/d}\right)}.

(K.1)

Proof K.2
.

The proof is deferred to
Section

N.1
.

The conclusion of
Theorem

K.1
is almost similar to Theorem 2 a) of
Kohler and Langer 2021
, but the authors of the paper did not explicitly state the magnitude bound
B
of network parameters, which is important in our application of variational deep learning. Due to this difference, we provide detailed proof for the sake of completeness.

For
Corollary

D.1
, we need the next lemma on neural network approximation of the composition structured functions.

Theorem K.3
(Approximation of composition structured functions).

Let
r\in\mathbbm{N}
,
\mathbf{d}=(d_{\ell})_{\ell\in[r]}
,
\bm{\beta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\beta_{\ell})_{\ell\in[r]}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{r}
,
\mathbf{s}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(s_{\ell})_{\ell\in[r]}\in\otimes_{\ell=1}^{r}[d_{\ell}]
and
F_{0}>0
. Then there exists absolute constants
M_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
,
K_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
,
\mathfrak{c}_{1}>0
,
\mathfrak{c}_{2}>0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{3}>0
such that for any
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq K_{0}}
,
M\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq M_{0}}
,
B>\mathfrak{c}_{1}\max_{\ell\in[r]}M^{\max\{1,2(\beta_{\ell}-1)/s_{\ell}\}}
and
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}^{\textsc{comp}}\mathinner{\left(r,\mathbf{d},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{s},F_{0}\right)}
, there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}^{*}\in\Theta^{\leq B}_{(K,M)}
such that

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-f^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\infty}\leq\mathfrak{c}_{2}\mathinner{\left(\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{3}K}+M^{-2\max_{\ell\in[r]}(\beta_{\geq\ell}/s_{\ell})}\right)},

(K.2)

where we let
\beta_{\geq\ell}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\beta_{\ell}\prod_{h=\ell+1}^{r}(\beta_{h}\wedge 1)
for
\ell\in[r-1]
and
\beta_{\geq r}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\beta_{r}
.

Proof K.4
.

The proof is deferred to
Section

N.2
.

K.1.2
Auxiliary results

In this subsection, we state the following two properties of the neural network model, which are used in the proofs.

Lemma K.5
(Lemma 9 of
Ohn and Kim 2022
).

Let
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
,
M\in\mathbbm{N}
and
B\geq 1
. Then for any
\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\Theta^{\leq B}_{(K,M)}
,

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})-\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2})\right\rVert}_{\infty}\leq K(B(M+1))^{K}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\bm{\theta}_{1}-\bm{\theta}_{2}\right\rvert}_{\infty}.

Lemma K.6
(Covering numbers of neural network classes).

For any
\zeta>0
,
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
,
M\in\mathbbm{N}
and
B\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\log\mathscr{N}\mathinner{\left(\zeta,\textup{{net}}\mathinner{\bigl(\Theta^{\leq B}_{(K,M)}\bigr)},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2KJ_{(K,M)}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{BK(M+1)}{\zeta}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2(d+1)K^{2}M^{2}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{BK(M+1)}{\zeta}\right)}.

Proof K.7
.

Since the dimension of the parameter space
\Theta^{\leq B}_{(K,M)}
is denoted by
J_{(K,M)}
, and is bounded as
J_{(K,M)}\leq(d+1)KM^{2}
, the desired result follows from the very well known result for the covering number bound of neural network classes, e.g., Lemma 3 of
Suzuki 2019
and Proposition A.1 of
Kim et al. 2021
.

K.2
Proofs of
Theorems

4.2
,
4.3
and
D.1

K.2.1
Proof of
Theorem

4.2

We first show that a suitable test function exists for the regression experiment in
Section

4.2
.

Lemma K.8
.

A sequence of the Gaussian regression experiments
\{(\mathbb{R}^{n},\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n};\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\textup{Ga}},\mathcal{F}^{d}))\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}
satisfies
B.1
and
B.2
with the metric
\mathscr{d}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{F}^{d}\times\mathcal{F}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
defined as
\mathscr{d}_{n}(f_{0},f_{1})=\mathinner{\lVert{f_{0}-f_{1}}\rVert}_{n,2}
for
f_{0},f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}^{d}
.

Proof K.9
.

The proof is deferred to
Section

K.3.1
.

Proof K.10
(Proof of
Theorem

4.2
).

We use
Lemma

B.1
to check
Item

A1
. First,
Lemma

K.8
verifies
Equations

B.1
and
B.2
. For
Equation

B.3
, we define

\zeta_{n,(K,M)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=KM\sqrt{\log n/n}.

Then by
Lemma

K.6
and the assumptions that
\max_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}}(K\vee M)\lesssim n
and
1\leq B_{n}\lesssim n^{\iota_{0}}
, there is an absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\zeta>\zeta_{n,(K,M)}}

\displaystyle\log\mathscr{N}\mathinner{\left(u\zeta,\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathinner{\lVert{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})-f^{\star}}\rVert}_{n,2}\leq 2\zeta\right\}},\|\cdot\|_{n,2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathscr{N}\mathinner{\left(u\zeta_{n,m},\textup{{net}}\mathinner{\left(\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)}\right)},\|\cdot\|_{n,2}\right)}\leq\log\mathscr{N}\mathinner{\left(u\zeta_{n,m},\textup{{net}}\mathinner{\left(\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)}\right)},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2(d+1)K^{2}M^{2}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{\mathfrak{c}_{1}n^{\nu+1/2}}{u}\right)}.

By using the inequality
(x+1)y\geq x+y
for any
x>0
and
y\geq 1
, the preceding display is further bounded by
c(u)K^{2}M^{2}\log n
with
c(u)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=2(d+1)(\nu+\frac{1}{2})\log(\e\fc_1/u)
for any
n\geq\textup{e}^{2}
, which verifies
B.3
.

We proceed to check
Item

A2
. Let

\eta_{n,(K,M)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sup_{\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}^{\star}}\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)}}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{f}\bigr\rVert}_{\infty}+n^{-1}.

Then by definition, for any
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}^{\star}
, there is a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{n,(K,M)}^{*}(f^{\star})\in\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)}
such that

\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{n,(K,M)}^{*}(f^{\star}))-f^{\star}}\bigr\rVert}_{n,2}\leq\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{n,(K,M)}^{*}(f^{\star}))-f^{\star}\bigr\rVert}_{\infty}\leq\eta_{n,(K,M)}.

To ease description, for each
(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
, let
\bm{\theta}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bm{\theta}_{n,(K,M)}^{*}(f^{\star})\in\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)}
and let
\bm{\theta}^{*}_{j}
be the
j
-th element of
\bm{\theta}^{*}
. Furthermore, let
\textit{Q}^{*}\in\mathcal{P}(\Theta^{\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M)})
be a distribution such that

\textit{Q}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{J_{(K,M)}}\texttt{{Unif}}\mathinner{\left((\theta_{j}^{*}-t_{n})\vee(-B_{n}),(\theta_{j}^{*}+t_{n})\wedge B_{n}\right)}

with
t_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\zeta_{n,(K,M)}\mathinner{(2K(B_{n}(M+1))^{K})}^{-1}
. Then, we have that

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}dnn\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}variational_{k}l}\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}_{f^{\star}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})}^{(n)}\right)}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\int\mathinner{\lVert{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})-f^{\star}}\rVert}_{n,2}^{2}\textup{d}\textit{Q}^{*}(\bm{\theta})

(K.3)

\displaystyle\leq n\int\mathinner{\lVert{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})-\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}\rVert}_{n,2}^{2}\textup{d}\textit{Q}^{*}(\bm{\theta})+n\eta^{2}_{n,(K,M)}

\displaystyle\leq n\int K^{2}(B_{n}(M+1))^{2K}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\rvert}_{\infty}^{2}\textup{d}\textit{Q}^{*}(\bm{\theta})+n\eta^{2}_{n,(K,M)}

\displaystyle\leq n\zeta^{2}_{n,(K,M)}+n\eta^{2}_{n,(K,M)}.

Furthermore, by the assumption that
(K\vee M\vee B_{n})\lesssim n^{\iota_{0}\vee 1}
and the inequality
J_{(K,M)}\leq(d+1)KM^{2}
,

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}^{*},\Pi_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{J_{(K,M)}}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{2B_{n}}{t_{n}}\right)}\lesssim(K(M)^{2})K\log n=2n\zeta_{n,(K,M)}^{2},

which verifies
Item

A2
.

Lastly,
B
follows from
|\mathcal{M}_{n}|\lesssim\exp(\fc_2\log n)
for some
\mathfrak{c}_{2}>0
and
Lemma

3.2
, so the proof is done.

K.2.2
Proof of
Corollaries

4.3
and
D.1

Proof K.11
(Proof of
Corollary

4.3
).

By
Theorem

K.1
, for any sufficiently large
n
, the convergence rate is given by

\epsilon_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}\bigr)}\asymp\min_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}}\mathinner{\left(2^{-K}+M^{-2\beta/d}+KM\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}\right)}.

Let
k_{n}^{*}\in\mathinner{[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\lceil(\log n)/2\rceil]}
be an integer such that

\left\lfloor{n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{\frac{d}{4\beta+2d}}\leq\left\lfloor{n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n},

and choose
K=\left\lfloor{\log n\times\log\log n}\right\rfloor
and
M=\lfloor n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}\rfloor
. Then since
\lfloor n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}\rfloor\geq\frac{1}{2}n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}
for any
n\geq\textup{e}^{2}
, and
n^{-1/\log n}=1/\textup{e}
, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\lesssim(n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n})^{-2\beta/d}+n^{\frac{d}{4\beta+2d}}\log n(\log\log n)\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{2}n^{\frac{d}{4\beta+2d}-1/\log n}\right)}^{-2\beta/d}+n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\log^{7/4}n

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\log^{7/4}n.

provided that

B_{n}\geq\sqrt{n}\geq n^{\max\mathinner{\left\{\frac{d}{4\beta+2d},\frac{\beta-1}{2\beta+d}\right\}}}\geq M^{\max\{1,2(\beta-1)/d\}}.

Taking
A_{n}=\log^{1/4}n
, we complete the proof.

Proof K.12
(Proof of
Corollary

D.1
).

For simplicity, let
t^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max_{\ell\in[r]}(\beta_{\geq\ell}/s_{\ell})
.
Let
k_{n}^{*}\in\mathinner{[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\lceil(\log n)/2\rceil]}
be an integer such that

\left\lfloor{n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{\frac{1}{4t^{*}+2}}\leq\left\lfloor{n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n},

and choose
K=\left\lfloor{\log\log n}\right\rfloor
and
M=\lfloor n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}\rfloor
. Then since
\lfloor n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}\rfloor\geq\frac{1}{2}n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}
for any
n\geq\textup{e}^{2}
, and
n^{-1/\log n}=1/\textup{e}
, we have, by
Theorem

K.3
,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathcal{F}^{\textup{DAG}}\mathinner{\left(r,\bm{\beta},\mathbf{s},F_{0}\right)}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\lesssim(n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n})^{-2t^{*}}+n^{\frac{1}{4t^{*}+2}}\log n(\log\log n)\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{2}n^{\frac{1}{4t^{*}+2}-1/\log n}\right)}^{-2t^{*}}+n^{-\frac{t^{*}}{2t^{*}+1}}\log^{7/4}n

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\frac{t^{*}}{2t^{*}+1}}\log^{7/4}n=n^{-\max_{\ell\in[r]}\frac{\beta_{\geq\ell}}{2\beta_{\geq\ell}+s_{\ell}}}\log^{7/4}n,

provided that

B_{n}\geq\sqrt{n}\geq\max_{\ell\in[r]}n^{\max\mathinner{\left\{\frac{s_{\ell}}{4\beta_{\ell}+2s_{\ell}},\frac{\beta_{\ell}-1}{2\beta_{\ell}+s_{\ell}}\right\}}}\geq\max_{\ell\in[r]}M^{\max\{1,2(\beta-1)/d\}}.

Taking
A_{n}=\log^{1/4}n
, we complete the proof.

K.3
Proofs of
Lemmas

K.8
,
D.6
and
D.3
: Checking
B.1
and
B.2
in
Lemma

B.1

K.3.1
Proof of
Lemma

K.8

Proof K.13
.

For any
\rho\in(0,1)\cup(1,\infty)
and any
f_{0},f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\textsf{{D}}_{\rho}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}_{f_{0}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{f_{1}}^{(n)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho-1}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\int\textup{e}^{-\frac{\rho-1}{2}\mathinner{\left((y_{i}-f_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}-(y_{i}-f_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}\right)}}\textup{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}(y_{i}-f_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\textup{d}y_{i}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho-1}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\int\textup{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathinner{\left(\rho(y_{i}-f_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}+(1-\rho)(y_{i}-f_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}\right)}}\textup{d}y_{i}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho-1}\log\mathinner{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textup{e}^{\frac{\rho(\rho-1)}{2}(f_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\textup{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\{y_{i}-(\rho f_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i})+(1-\rho)f_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))\}^{2}}\textup{d}y_{i}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\mathinner{\left(\textup{e}^{\frac{\rho(\rho-1)}{2}(f_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho n}{2}\mathinner{\lVert{f_{0}-f_{1}}\rVert}_{n,2}^{2}=\frac{\rho}{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(f_{0},f_{1}),

which completes the proof

K.3.2
Proof of
Lemma

D.3

Proof K.14
.

We prove the desired result by verifying the two conditions of
Lemma

B.3
. For notational convenience, we denote the likelihood ratio by

\displaystyle\textsf{{r}}(p_{0},p_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textsf{{r}}_{n}(p_{0},p_{1};\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\text{Ber}}(p_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\text{Ber}}(p_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}

\displaystyle=\exp\mathinner{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\left\{Y_{i}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{p_{0}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}{p_{1}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}\right)}+(1-Y_{i})\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1-p_{0}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}{1-p_{1}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}\right)}\right\}}\right)}.

The KL divergence from
\textsf{{P}}_{p_{0}}^{(n)}
to
\textsf{{P}}_{p_{1}}^{(n)}
is given by

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textsf{{P}}_{p_{0}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{p_{1}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{p_{0}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\log\textsf{{r}}(p_{0},p_{1})\right]}

\displaystyle=n\int\mathinner{\left\{p_{0}(\mathbf{x})\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{p_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{p_{1}(\mathbf{x})}\right)}+(1-p_{0}(\mathbf{x}))\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1-p_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{1-p_{1}(\mathbf{x})}\right)}\right\}}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}.

Let
D_{w_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[\varkappa,1-\varkappa]\mapsto\mathbb{R}
for fixed
w_{0}\in[\varkappa,1-\varkappa]
be a function such that
D_{w_{0}}(w)=w_{0}\log(w_0/w)-(1-w_{0})\log((1-w_0)/(1-w))
. Then by the Taylor expansion at
w_{0}
, we have

D_{w_{0}}(w)=\frac{1}{2}D^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}(\widetilde{w})(w-w_{0})^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\mathinner{\left(\frac{w_{0}}{\widetilde{w}^{2}}+\frac{1-w_{0}}{(1-\widetilde{w})^{2}}\right)}(w-w_{0})^{2}

for some
\widetilde{w}
that lies between
w
and
w_{0}
. Since
\varkappa/(1-\varkappa)^{2}\leq D^{\prime\prime}_{w_{0}}\leq(1-\varkappa)/\varkappa^{2}
on
[\varkappa,1-\varkappa]
, the first condition of
Lemma

B.3
, which assumes the equivalence between the KL divergence and the metric
\mathscr{d}_{n}
, holds. For the second condition of
Lemma

B.3
, we first use Hoeffding’s lemma to obtain

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}logit_{g}auss}\textsf{{P}}_{p_{0}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{t\mathinner{\left\{\log\textsf{{r}}(p_{0},p_{1})-\textup{KL}(\textsf{{P}}_{p_{0}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{p_{1}}^{(n)})\right\}}}\right]}\leq\mathinner{\left\{\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\textup{e}^{(t^{2}/8)(\text{logit}(p_{0}(\mathbf{x}))-\text{logit}(p_{1}(\mathbf{x})))^{2}}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}\right\}}^{n},

(K.4)

where we write
\text{logit}(w)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\log(w/(1-w))
for
w\in[0,1]
. Note that for any
p_{0},p_{1}\in[\varkappa,1-\varkappa]

\displaystyle(\text{logit}(p_{0})-\text{logit}(p_{1}))^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\left\{\log\mathinner{\left(1+\frac{p_{0}-p_{1}}{(1-p_{0})p_{1}}\right)}\right\}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\varkappa^{4}}(p_{0}-p_{1})^{2}.

Thus, for any
|t|\leq\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}_{0}(8\varkappa^{4})}
with
\mathfrak{c}_{0}>0
arbitrarily chosen, the right-hand side of
K.4
is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathinner{\left\{\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\textup{e}^{t^{2}/(8\varkappa^{4})(p_{0}(\mathbf{x})-p_{1}(\mathbf{x}))^{2}}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}\right\}}^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\left\{\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\mathinner{\left\{1+(\textup{e}^{t^{2}/(8\varkappa^{4})}-1)(p_{0}(\mathbf{x})-p_{1}(\mathbf{x}))^{2}\right\}}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}\right\}}^{n}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\left\{1+(\textup{e}^{t^{2}/(8\varkappa^{4})}-1)\|p_{0}-p_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}}^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\left\{1+(\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}-1)t^{2}\|p_{0}-p_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\right\}}^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{(\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{0}}-1)t^{2}n\|p_{0}-p_{1}\|_{2}^{2}},

which verifies the second condition of
Lemma

B.3
.

K.3.3
Proof of
Lemma

D.6

Proof K.15
.

Let
\mathscr{I}(\lambda)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\lambda(\mathbf{x})\textup{d}\mathbf{x}
. Note first that

\displaystyle\textsf{{D}}_{2}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\log\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{1})-\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{0})}\textsf{{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{\int\log(\lambda_0/\lambda_1)\textup{d}Y}\right]}\right\}}^{n},

where
Y\sim\textsf{{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}
. But since

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{\int\log(\lambda_0/\lambda_1)\textup{d}Y}\right]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\textup{e}^{-\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{0})}\frac{1}{N!}\mathinner{\left\{\int\frac{\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})}{\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})}\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})\textup{d}\mathbf{x}\right\}}^{N}

\displaystyle=\exp\mathinner{\left(-\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{0})+\int\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}(\mathbf{x})}{\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}\right)},

we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{D}}_{2}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle=n\int\mathinner{\left\{\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}(\mathbf{x})}{\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})}-2\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})+\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})\right\}}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=n\int\mathinner{\left\{\frac{1}{\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})}(\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})-\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x}))^{2}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{\varkappa_{\min}}\|\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}\|_{2}^{2},

which proves
B.2
. For
B.1
, we have that by the similar calculation as before

\displaystyle\textsf{{D}}_{1/2}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{0}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)}

\displaystyle=-2\log\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\lambda_{0}}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\text{PPP}}(\lambda_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\text{PPP}}(\lambda_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}^{1/2}\right]}

\displaystyle=-2\log\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{1})+\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{0})}\textsf{{P}}_{\lambda_{0}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{\int\log(\lambda_1/\lambda_0)^{1/2}\textup{d}Y}\right]}\right\}}^{n}

\displaystyle=n\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{1})-n\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{0})+2n\mathscr{I}(\lambda_{0})+2n\int\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})^{1/2}\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})^{1/2}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=n\int\mathinner{\left(\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})^{1/2}-\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})^{1/2}\right)}^{2}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=n\int\frac{1}{(\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})^{1/2}+\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})^{1/2})^{2}}\mathinner{\left(\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{x})-\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{x})\right)}^{2}\textup{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n}{4\varkappa_{\max}}\|\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}\|_{2}^{2},

which completes the proof.

Appendix L
Proofs of results for combinatorial model spaces in
Section

5
and

F

L.1
Proof for
Section

5.1

L.1.1
Proof of
Theorem

5.1

Proof L.1
.

The proof is basically the same as those of
Theorems

3.4
,
3.8
and
3.9
. The only difference, which lies in
Equation

5.7
and arises from the weakened assumption
5.4
, is to bound the type-I error of the test function

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max_{(m,S)\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\varphi_{n,m,S}

where
(\varphi_{n,m,S})_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n},S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}
are the test functions given in
Item

D1
and we define, for sufficiently large
H_{1}>1
and given
\bar{\epsilon}\geq\epsilon_{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,m,S}\leq H_{1}\bar{\epsilon}\right\}}.

Then, by the assumption
5.4
,
J.10
in the proof of
Theorem

J.8
used in the proof of
Theorem

3.4
is replaced with

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(m,S)\in\mathcal{V}_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n,m,S}]

(L.1)

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathcal{V}_{n}\right\rvert}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n(A_{0}/J_{0})^{2}\bar{\epsilon}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{3}n(H_{1}\bar{\epsilon})^{2}-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n(A_{0}/J_{0})^{2}\bar{\epsilon}^{2}}.

The rest of the proof of
5.7
as well as the proofs of
5.9
and
5.8
are almost similar, so we omit them.

L.2
Proofs for
Section

5.2

We give a technical lemma used in the proofs. For a matrix
\mathbf{L}
, we let
\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sqrt{\Tr(\L^\top\L)}
denote its Frobenius norm.

Lemma L.2
.

Let
\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}+\mathbf{I}\in\Lambda_{n}^{\star}
. Then for any
\bm{\Sigma}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbf{L}\mathbf{L}^{\top}+\mathbf{I}
,

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}}^{(n)})\leq\frac{n}{4}\mathinner{\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}-\bm{\Sigma}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}.

(L.2)

Proof L.3
.

Let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{1},\dots,\dots\widetilde{\sigma}_{d_{n}}
be the eigenvalues of
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}
. Then

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\mathinner{\left\{\Tr(\bSigma^\star\bSigma^{-1})+\log|\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}|-1\right\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\mathinner{\left\{\Tr((\bSigma^\star)^{1/2}\bSigma^{-1} (\bSigma^\star)^{1/2})+\log|(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}|-1\right\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{n}}\mathinner{\left\{\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}-1+\log(\tsigma_j)\right\}}\leq\frac{n}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{n}}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}-1)^{2},

where the last inequality follows from the inequality
z-\log(1+z)\leq z^{2}/2
for any
z\geq 0
. We complete the proof by noting that

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}sparse\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}factor\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}eigensumeq}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{n}}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}-1)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert{(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}-\mathbf{I}}\bigr\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}=\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}-\mathbf{I}}\bigr\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}

(L.3)

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}}\bigr\rVert}_{\textup{op}}^{2}\mathinner{\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}-\bm{\Sigma}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\mathinner{\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}-\bm{\Sigma}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2},

where the second equality holds due to the fact that the two matrices
(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}(\bm{\Sigma}^{\star})^{1/2}
and
\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}
have the same set of non-zero eigenvalues by similarity and the last inequality follows from that
\mathinner{\lVert\bm{\Sigma}^{-1}\rVert}_{\text{op}}\leq 1
.

L.2.1
Proof of
Theorem

5.3

Proof L.4
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbbm{P}([d_{n}])\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{S\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S\subset[d_{n}]\}
.
Fix
\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}=\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})\in\Lambda_{n}^{\star}
. We first verify the testing condition of
Item

D1
with

\displaystyle\zeta_{n,m,S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sqrt{(|S|\vee s_{n})m\log d_{n}/n}\mbox{ for }(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}.

Here, we use the information of the true sparsity
s_{n}
, because the test we shall use depends on
s_{n}
. Namely, by Lemma 5.7 of
Gao and Zhou 2015
, there exists a test function
\varphi_{n,m,S}
such that

\displaystyle\max\mathinner{\left\{\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)}[\varphi_{n,m,S}],\sup_{\bm{\Sigma}\in\Theta_{n,m,S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathinner{\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}-\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{op}}>J_{0}\zeta}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}}^{(n)}[1-\varphi_{n,m,S}]\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{c}_{1}(|S|+s_{n}-n\zeta^{2})\right)}

for any
\zeta>0
for some absolute constants
\mathfrak{c}_{1}>0
and
J_{0}>0
. Hence,
Item

D1
is satisfied since the testing error of
\varphi_{n,m,S}
is exponentially bounded whenever
\zeta>\zeta_{n,m,S}\gtrsim\sqrt{(|S|+s_{n})/n}
.

We proceed to verify
Item

D2
. Although
Item

D2
requires that every individual model and the prior satisfy the given condition, one can notice that it suffices to prove it only for
m=r_{n}
and
S=S^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textup{supp}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})
because the optimal rate can be attained by the model with
m=r_{n}
and
S=S^{\star}
and we can make
Item

D2
hold for any other individual models by letting
\eta_{n,m,S}=\infty.
For
r_{n}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
and
S^{\star}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}
, we can set
\eta_{n,r_{n},S^{\star}}=0
since
\mathbf{L}^{\star}\in\Theta_{n,r_{n},S^{\star}}
. We then have
\epsilon_{n}=\eta_{n,r_{n},S^{\star}}+\zeta_{n,r_{n},S^{\star}}=\sqrt{s_{n}r_{n}\log d_{n}/n}
. Let

\displaystyle\textit{Q}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bigotimes_{j\notin S^{\star}}\delta(\cdot;\mathbf{0}_{r_{n}})\times\bigotimes_{j\in S^{\star}}\bigotimes_{k=1}^{r_{n}}\texttt{{N}}(L^{\star}_{j,k},\tau_{0})\in\mathcal{P}(\Theta_{n,r_{n}}).

Note that for any
\mathbf{L}\in\Theta_{n,r_{n},S^{\star}}
,

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}sparse\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}factor\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}fnorm_{b}ound}\mathinner{\lVert{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}-\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L})}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{L}^{\top}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}

(L.4)

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+2\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+2\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{op}}\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+2\sqrt{\bar{\sigma}}\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}.

Therefore, by
Lemma

L.2
and Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L})}^{(n)}\right)}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{4}\mathinner{\Bigl(\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+2\sqrt{\bar{\sigma}}\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}\right]}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{4}\mathinner{\biggl(\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right]}+2\sqrt{\bar{\sigma}}\sqrt{\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right]}}\biggr)}.

Since
\textit{Q}^{*}(L_{j,k})=\texttt{{N}}(L_{j,k}^{\star},\tau_{0})
for
j\in S^{\star}
, the above display is further bounded as

\displaystyle\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L})}^{(n)}\right)}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{4}\mathinner{\left(s_{n}r_{n}\tau_{0}+2\sqrt{\bar{\sigma}}\sqrt{s_{n}r_{n}\tau_{0}}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim ns_{n}r_{n}\leq n\zeta_{n,r_{n},S^{\star}}^{2}.

We bound the KL divergence from the prior
\Pi_{n,r_{n}}
to
\textit{Q}^{*}
. We use the following equations for the KL divergence between two spike-and-slab distributions,

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}sparse\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}kl_{s}pikeslab}\textup{KL}((1-\omega_{1})

\displaystyle\delta(\cdot;\mathbf{0})+\omega_{1}\textit{Q}_{1},(1-\omega_{0})\delta(\cdot;\mathbf{0})+\omega_{0}\textit{Q}_{0})

(L.5)

\displaystyle=(1-\omega_{1})\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1-\omega_{1}}{1-\omega_{0}}\right)}+\omega_{1}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{\omega_{1}}{\omega_{0}}\right)}+\omega_{1}\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{1},\textit{Q}_{0})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{1-\omega_{0}}\right)}&\mbox{ if }\omega_{1}=0\\
\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{0}}\right)}+\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{1},\textit{Q}_{0})&\mbox{ if }\omega_{1}=1\end{cases}

which holds for any
\omega_{0}\in(0,1)
. By this, we have

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}^{*},\Pi_{n,r_{n}})

\displaystyle\leq(d_{n}-|S^{\star}|)\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{1-\omega_{n,r_{n}}}\right)}+|S^{\star}|\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{n,r_{n}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}\sum_{k=1}^{r_{n}}\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\texttt{{N}}(L_{j,k}^{\star},\tau_{0}),\texttt{{N}}(0,\tau_{0})\right)}.

We separately bound the three terms in the above display.
The first term vanishes since

\displaystyle(1-\omega_{n,r_{n}})^{d_{n}-|S^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\geq\textup{e}^{-\omega_{n,r_{n}}(d_{n}-|S^{\star}|)}

\displaystyle\geq\exp\mathinner{\bigl(-\textup{e}^{\log d_{n}-(1+\mathfrak{a}_{0})r_{n}\log d_{n}}\bigr)}\to 1,

as
n\to\infty
. The second term is bounded as

\displaystyle|S^{\star}|\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{n,r_{n}}}\right)}\leq(1+\mathfrak{a}_{0})s_{n}r_{n}\log d_{n}

and the third term is as

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}\sum_{k=1}^{r_{n}}\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\texttt{{N}}(L_{j,k}^{\star},\tau_{0}),\texttt{{N}}(0,\tau_{0})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in S^{\star}}\sum_{k=1}^{r_{n}}\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}(L_{j,k}^{\star})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau_{0}}\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{r_{n}}{2\tau_{0}}\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{op}}^{2}\lesssim r_{n}.

Combining all the derived results, we can see that
Item

D2
is satisfied.

It remains to check
Item

D3
. First, since
\alpha_{n,S|m}=\omega_{n,m}^{|S|}(1-\omega_{n,m})^{d_{n}-|S|}\leq\omega_{n,m}^{|S|}
, we have

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}sparse\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}priorbound_{l}argemodels}\sum_{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,m,S}\geq H\epsilon_{n}}\alpha_{n,m}\alpha_{n,S|m}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}}\alpha_{n,m}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,m,S}\geq H\epsilon_{n}}\alpha_{n,S|m}

(L.6)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m\geq r_{n}}\alpha_{n,m}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|S|\geq H^{2}s_{n}}\alpha_{n,S|m}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m\geq r_{n}}\alpha_{n,m}\sum_{s\in[d_{n}]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}s\geq H^{2}s_{n}}\binom{d_{n}}{s}\textup{e}^{-(1+\mathfrak{a}_{0})sm\log d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m\geq r_{n}}\alpha_{n,m}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{0}H^{2}s_{n}m\log d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{\log n}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{0}H^{2}s_{n}r_{n}\log d_{n}}\leq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{3}H^{2}s_{n}r_{n}\log d_{n}}

for some absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{3}>0
, which verifies
5.5
. Also,
5.4
is met because

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,m,S}<H\epsilon_{n}\right\}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{M}_{n}|\times\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathinner{\left\{S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|S|<H^{2}s_{n}\right\}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{\log n}d_{n}^{H^{2}s_{n}+1}\leq\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{4}H^{2}s_{n}\log d_{n}}

for some absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{4}>0
.
Lastly
5.6
is verified as

\displaystyle\alpha_{n,r_{n}}\alpha_{n,S^{\star}|r_{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\omega_{n,r_{n}}^{|S^{\star}|}(1-\omega_{n,r_{n}})^{d_{n}-|S^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\textup{e}^{-\log n}\omega_{n,r_{n}}^{|S^{\star}|}\geq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{5}s_{n}r_{n}\log d_{n}}

for some absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{5}>0
, which completes the proof.

L.2.2
Proof of
Theorem

5.4

In the following proof, we only prove the first result
5.13
on the factor dimensionality. The second result
5.14
can be proved by the exactly same argument.

Proof L.5
.

We first consider underestimation of the true factor dimensionality
r_{n}
. For any
\mathbf{L}\in\Theta_{n,m}
with
m<r_{n}
, since
\mathbf{L}^{\star}
is of full rank, there is a vector
\mathbf{x}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{n}}
with
|\mathbf{x}_{1}|_{2}=1
such that
(\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{1}\neq 0
but
\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{1}=0
. This implies that

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathbf{L}\in\Theta_{n,m}}\mathinner{\lVert{\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{L})-\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}\rVert}_{\textup{op}}\geq\sigma_{r_{n}}\mathinner{\left(\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}\right)}\geq\eta_{n}^{*}.

Thus,
5.8
in
Theorem

5.1
leads to the desired.

For the overestimation problem, we start with the bound such as

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)}\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}\mathinner{\left(\mathinner{\left\{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m>H_{1}r_{n}\right\}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)}\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}\mathinner{\left(\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m>H_{1}r_{n},|S|<s_{n}\right\}}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Sigma}^{\star}}^{(n)}\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}\mathinner{\left(\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m>H_{1}r_{n},|S|\geq s_{n}\right\}}\right)},

where we let
\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbbm{P}([d_{n}])
.
We first bound the first term in the above display. For any
\mathbf{L}\in\Theta_{n,m,S}
with
|S|<s_{n}
, since there exists an index
j^{*}\in S^{\star}\setminus S
, we have that for a vector
\mathbf{x}_{2}
of 0’s except the
j^{*}
-th element equal to 1,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\lVert{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{L}^{\top}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}}\rVert}_{\textup{op}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathbf{L}\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{2}-\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}^{\star})^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{2}\right\rvert}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mathinner{\!\bigl\lvert\mathbf{L}^{\star}(\mathbf{L}^{\star}_{j,\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}})^{\top}\bigr\rvert}\geq\mathinner{\!\bigl\lvert\mathbf{L}^{\star}_{j,\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}}\bigr\rvert}_{2}^{2},

which is larger than
\eta_{n}^{*}
by assumption, so the first term vanishes. For the second term also converges to zero, since we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{n}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\left(\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\in\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m>H_{1}r_{n},|S|\geq s_{n}\right\}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\left(\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}m|S|>H_{1}s_{n}r_{n},|S|\geq s_{n}\right\}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\left(\mathinner{\left\{(m,S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,m,S}>H_{1}\epsilon_{n}\right\}}\right)}\leq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{3}H_{1}^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}},

where the last inequality was established already in
L.6
in the proof of
Theorem

5.3
.

L.3
Proof for
Appendix

F

L.3.1
Proof of
Theorem

F.1

Proof L.6
.

Before giving the proof, we introduce additional notation. For
\bm{\theta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=((\mathbf{W}_{k},\mathbf{b}_{k}))_{k\in[K]}\in\Theta_{(K,M)}^{\leq B_{n}}
, we define its “input” support

\displaystyle\textup{supp}_{1}(\bm{\theta})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{j\in[d_{n}]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathbf{W}_{1,j,\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}}\right\rvert}_{0}>0\right\}}.

Then the cardinality of
\textup{supp}_{1}(\bm{\theta})
is equivalent to the number of input variables that affect the output of the neural network
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})
. We consider a subset of each individual network parameter space with an input sparsity constraint, which is defined as

\displaystyle\Theta_{(K,M),S}^{d_{n},\leq B_{n}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{(K,M)}^{d_{n},\leq B_{n}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\textup{supp}_{1}(\bm{\theta})=S\right\}}

and

\displaystyle J_{(K,M),S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\Theta_{(K,M),S}^{d_{n},\leq B_{n}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle=(|S|+1)M+(K-2)(M^{2}+M)+(M+1)

\displaystyle\leq|S|M+2KM^{2}

for each
S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbbm{P}([d_{n}])
.

We first show the conditions in
Lemma

B.1
are satisfied to check
Item

D1
. First
B.1
and
B.2
are met by
Lemma

K.8
. Moreover, by
Lemma

K.6
, we have

\displaystyle\log\mathscr{N}\mathinner{\left(n^{-1},\textup{{net}}\mathinner{\bigl(\Theta^{\textup{d}_{n},leB}_{(K,M),S}\bigr)},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 2(|S|KM+2K^{2}M^{2})\log(nB_nK(M+1))

\displaystyle\lesssim(|S|KM+K^{2}M^{2})\log n

where the last line follows from the assumption that
\max_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}}(K\vee M)\lesssim n
and
1\leq B_{n}\lesssim n^{\iota_{0}}
. Therefore, the condition
B.3
in
Lemma

B.1
is met with

\displaystyle\zeta_{n,(K,M),S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{n}\mathinner{\left\{(|S|KM+K^{2}M^{2})\log n+|S|\log d_{n}\right\}}\right)}^{1/2},

where the term
|S|\log d_{n}
is additionally introduced to control the complexity arising from the variable selection.

Now we proceed to check
Item

D2
. Let

\displaystyle\eta_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sup_{\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}^{\textup{sparse}}\mathinner{\left(d_{n},\beta,s_{0},F_{0}\right)}}\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{d_{n},\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M),S^{\star}}}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})-\widetilde{f}\bigr\rVert}_{\infty}+n^{-1}

and let
\eta_{n,(K,M),S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\infty
for
S\neq S^{\star}
. By this trick, we can simply check
Item

D2
with the upper bound
n(\eta_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}+\zeta_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}})^{2}
. For each
(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
, by slightly abusing a notation, let
\bm{\theta}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bm{\theta}^{*}_{(K,M),S^{\star}}\in\Theta^{d_{n},\leq B_{n}}_{(K,M),S^{\star}}
be a network parameter such that
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})-f^{\star}\right\rVert}_{\infty}\leq\eta_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}
. Also, let
\textit{Q}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textit{Q}^{*}_{(K,M),S^{\star}}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n,(K,M)}
be a distribution such that

\displaystyle\textit{Q}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bigotimes_{h=1}^{d_{n}}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\Bigl\{(1-\nu_{h}^{*})\delta(\cdot;\mathbf{0}_{M+1})+\nu_{h}^{*}\bigotimes_{j=(h-1)M+1}^{hM}\texttt{{Unif}}(-(\theta_{j}^{*}-t_{n})\vee(-B_{n}),(\theta_{j}^{*}+t_{n})\wedge B_{n})\Bigr\}}

\displaystyle\times\bigotimes_{j=d_{n}M+1}^{J_{n,(K,M)}}\texttt{{Unif}}(-(\theta_{j}^{*}-t_{n})\vee(-B_{n}),(\theta_{j}^{*}+t_{n})\wedge B_{n}),

where
\nu_{h}^{*}=1
if
h\in S^{\star}
and
\nu_{h}^{*}=0
otherwise,
\theta_{j}^{*}
denotes the
j
-th element of
\bm{\theta}^{*}
and we let
t_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\zeta_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}\mathinner{(2K(B_{n}(M+1))^{K})}^{-1}
. Then by a similar argument to
K.3
in the proof of
Theorem

4.2
, we have

\displaystyle\textit{Q}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{P}}_{f^{\star}}^{(n)},\textsf{{P}}_{\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})}^{(n)}\bigr)}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq n\zeta^{2}_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}+n\eta^{2}_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}.

But bounding the KL divergence between
\textit{Q}^{*}
and the prior
\Pi_{n,(K,M)}
is more involved. We use
L.5
that was given in the proof of
Theorem

5.3
to obtain

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}^{*},\Pi_{n,(K,M)})

\displaystyle\leq(d_{n}-|S^{\star}|)\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{1-\omega_{n,(K,M)}}\right)}+|S^{\star}|\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{n,(K,M)}}\right)}+J_{(K,M),S^{\star}}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{2B_{n}}{t_{n}}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\log\mathinner{\bigl((1-\omega_{n,(K,M)})^{d_{n}-|S^{\star}|}\omega_{n,(K,M)}^{|S^{\star}|}\bigr)}+J_{(K,M),S^{\star}}\log n

\displaystyle\lesssim-|S^{\star}|\log\mathinner{\bigl(\omega_{n,(K,M)}\bigr)}+n\zeta^{2}_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|S^{\star}|(KM\log n+\log d_{n})+n\zeta^{2}_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\zeta^{2}_{n,(K,M),S^{\star}},

where the third inequality follows from

\displaystyle(1-\omega_{n,(K^{*},M^{*})})^{d_{n}-|S^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\geq\textup{e}^{-\omega_{n,(K^{*},M^{*})}(d_{n}-|S^{\star}|)}

\displaystyle\geq\exp\mathinner{\bigl(-\textup{e}^{\log d_{n}-\mathfrak{a}_{1}K^{*}M^{*}\log n-(1+\mathfrak{a}_{2})\log d_{n}}\bigr)}\to 1.

Hence,
Item

D2
is satisfied.

It remains to check
Item

D3
. Let

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+s_{0}}}\log^{7/4}n+\sqrt{\frac{\log d_{n}}{n}}.

For
5.5
, since

\displaystyle\alpha_{n,S|(K,M)}

\displaystyle=w_{n,(K,M)}^{|S|}(1-w_{n,(K,M)})^{d_{n}-|S|}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{1}|S|KM\log n-(1+\mathfrak{a}_{2})|S|\log d_{n}},

we have

\displaystyle\sum_{((K,M),S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,(K,M),S}\geq H\epsilon_{n}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\alpha_{n,S|(K,M)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n\geq H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}+\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|S|\geq\underline{s}_{n,(K,M)}}\alpha_{n,S|(K,M)}

\displaystyle\quad\leq|\mathcal{M}_{n}|\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{3}H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}+\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|S|\geq\underline{s}_{n,(K,M)}}\alpha_{n,S|(K,M)}.

where we let

\displaystyle\underline{s}_{n,(K,M)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}-K^{2}M^{2}\log n}{KM\log n+\log d_{n}}.

We proceed to

\displaystyle\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|S|\geq\underline{s}_{n,(K,M)}}\alpha_{n,S|(K,M)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\sum_{s\in[d_{n}]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}s\geq\underline{s}_{n,(K,M)}}\binom{d_{n}}{s}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{1}sKM\log n-(1+\mathfrak{a}_{2})s\log d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\sum_{s\in[d_{n}]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}s\geq\underline{s}_{n,(K,M)}}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{1}sKM\log n-\mathfrak{a}_{2}s\log d_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\alpha_{n,(K,M)}\textup{e}^{-(\mathfrak{a}_{1}\wedge\mathfrak{a}_{2})(H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}-K^{2}M^{2}\log n)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}K^{2}M^{2}\log n<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\frac{1}{Z_{\alpha,n}}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{1}\wedge\mathfrak{a}_{2}H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}-(\mathfrak{a}_{3}-\mathfrak{a}_{1}\wedge\mathfrak{a}_{2})K^{2}M^{2}\log n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{2}H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}

for some constant
\mathfrak{c}_{2}>0
, where the last line follows from

\displaystyle Z_{\alpha,n}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{3}K^{2}M^{2}\log n}

\displaystyle\geq\exp\mathinner{\bigl(-\mathfrak{a}_{3}\log n\min_{(K,M)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}}(KM)^{2}\bigr)}\geq\exp(-2\fa_3\log n)

and the assumption
\mathfrak{a}_{3}>\mathfrak{a}_{1}\wedge\mathfrak{a}_{2}
. Next,
5.4
can be verified as

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathinner{\left\{((K,M),S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{n,(K,M),S}<H\epsilon_{n}\right\}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathinner{\left\{((K,M),S)\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\times\mathcal{S}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|S|\log d_{n}<H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}\right\}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{M}_{n}|d_{n}^{H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}/\log d_{n}+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\textup{e}^{H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}+\log d_{n}+\log n}\leq\textup{e}^{2H^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}.

Lastly, for
5.6
, we prove the existence of a “best” network architecture
(K^{*},M^{*})
that satisfies
\eta_{n,(K^{*},M^{*}),S^{\star}}+\zeta_{n,(K^{*},M^{*}),S^{\star}}\lesssim\epsilon_{n}
. Let
k_{n}^{*}\in\mathinner{[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\lceil(\log n)/2\rceil]}
be an integer such that

\left\lfloor{n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{\frac{s_{0}}{4\beta+2s_{0}}}\leq\left\lfloor{n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n},

and choose
K^{*}=\left\lfloor{\log n\times\log\log n}\right\rfloor
and
M^{*}=\lfloor n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}\rfloor
so that

\displaystyle\zeta_{n,(K^{*},M^{*}),S^{\star}}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{n}\mathinner{\left\{(s_{0}K^{*}M^{*}+(K^{*}M^{*})^{2})\log n+s_{0}\log d_{n}\right\}}\right)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim K^{*}M^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log d_{n}}{n}}\lesssim\epsilon_{n}

and that by
Theorem

K.1
,

\displaystyle\eta_{n,(K^{*},M^{*}),S^{\star}}\lesssim n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{n}.

For such
K^{*}
and
M^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{n,(K^{*},M^{*})}\alpha_{n,S^{\star}|(K^{*},M^{*})}

\displaystyle=Z_{\alpha,n}^{-1}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{3}(K^{*}M^{*})^{2}\log n}(1-\omega_{n,(K^{*},M^{*})})^{d_{n}-|S^{\star}|}\omega_{n,(K^{*},M^{*})}^{|S^{\star}|}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{a}_{3}(K^{*}M^{*})^{2}\log n-\mathfrak{a}_{1}s_{0}K^{*}M^{*}\log n-(1+\mathfrak{a}_{2})s_{0}\log d_{n}}\gtrsim\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{3}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}

for some constant
\mathfrak{c}_{3}>0
, where the second inequality holds due to
Z_{\alpha,n}\leq|\mathcal{M}_{n}|\lesssim(\log n)\log\log n
. This completes the proof.

Appendix M
Additional results and proofs for adaptive variational quasi-posteriors in
Section

7
and
Appendix

I

M.1
Factional likelihoods in our framework

In this subsection, we show that fractional likelihoods satisfy
Item

E1
.

Proposition M.1
.

When the conditions
B.1
and
B.2
in
Lemma

B.1
hold for a likelihood function
\textsf{{p}}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\Lambda_{n}\times\mathbbm{Y}_{n}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, then its fractional version
(\textsf{{p}}_{n})^{\kappa}
satisfies
Item

E1
for any
\kappa\in(0,1)
.

Proof M.2
.

We first verify
7.6
.
Since either
\kappa>\rho_{\circ}
or
(1-\kappa)\geq 1-\rho_{\circ}
holds, by
B.1
, we have either

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\frac{(\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))^{\kappa}}{(\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))^{\kappa}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}^{\kappa-1}\right]}

\displaystyle=\exp\mathinner{\left(-(1-\kappa)\textsf{{D}}_{\kappa}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-(1-\kappa)\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\circ}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-(1-\kappa)\mathfrak{c}_{1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right)}

or

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\frac{(\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))^{\kappa}}{(\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))^{\kappa}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}^{-\kappa}\right]}

\displaystyle=\exp\mathinner{\left(-\kappa\textsf{{D}}_{1-\kappa}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-\kappa\textsf{{D}}_{1-\rho_{\circ}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-\kappa\frac{1-\rho_{\circ}}{\rho_{\circ}}\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\circ}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left(-\kappa\frac{1-\rho_{\circ}}{\rho_{\circ}}\mathfrak{c}_{1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right)}.

This implies
7.6
. Moreover,
7.7
is met with
\rho=(\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)/\kappa>0
, as

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{(\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))^{\kappa}}{(\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))^{\kappa}}\right)}^{\rho}\right]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}^{\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1}\right]}

\displaystyle=\exp\mathinner{\left((\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)\textsf{{D}}_{\rho_{\blacklozenge}}(\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}},\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\mathinner{\left((\rho_{\blacklozenge}-1)\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\bm{\lambda})\right)}

where the inequality follows from
B.2
.

M.2
Proofs for
Section

7.2

M.2.1
Proof of
Theorem

7.1

Proof M.3
.

We first prove
7.9
.
Let
\textsf{{p}}_{n,\Pi_{n}}^{\natural}(\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Pi_{n}[\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})]
. For any
\textit{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n}
, by Fubini’s theorem, we get

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}quasi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}vgap\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}klexpansion}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))\right]}

(M.1)

\displaystyle=\textit{Q}\mathinner{\Biggl[\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\Biggl[\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n,\Pi_{n}}^{\natural}(\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\textup{d}\textit{Q}}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\textup{d}\Pi_{n}}\biggr)}\Biggr]}\Biggr]}

\displaystyle=\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi_{n})+\textit{Q}\mathinner{\Biggl[\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\Biggl[\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr)}\Biggr]}\Biggr]}+\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\Biggl[\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n,\Pi_{n}}^{\natural}(\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr)}\Biggr]}.

By Jensen’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem and the assumption
7.6

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}quasi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}vgap\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}third}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n,\Pi_{n}}^{\natural}(\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr)}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathinner{\Biggl(\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n,\Pi_{n}}^{\natural}(\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr]}\Biggr)}

(M.2)

\displaystyle=\log\mathinner{\Biggl(\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\Biggl[\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\biggl[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr]}\Biggr]}\Biggr)}

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathinner{\Bigl(\Pi_{n}\mathinner{\bigl[\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})}\bigr]}\Bigr)}\leq 0

Moreover, by using Jensen’s inequality and the assumption
7.7
,

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}quasi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}vgap\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}second}\textit{Q}\mathinner{\Biggl[\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr)}\Biggr]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\textit{Q}\mathinner{\Biggl[\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\log\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr)}^{\rho}\Biggr]}

(M.3)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho}\textit{Q}\mathinner{\Biggl[\log\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\biggl[\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\biggr)}^{\rho}\biggr]}\Biggr]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho}\textit{Q}\mathinner{\left[\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right]}.

Substituting
M.2
and
M.3
in the last line of
M.1
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}(\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n},\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))\right]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\inf_{\textit{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n}}\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\textit{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\textit{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n}}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi_{n})+\frac{1}{\rho}\textit{Q}\mathinner{\left[\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right]}\right\}}.

Then the proof is complete by the fact that
\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{m},\Pi_{n})=-\log(\alpha_{n,m})+\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{m},\Pi_{n,m})
for any
\textit{Q}_{m}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n,m}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
.

We proceed to prove the second assertion on
7.5
. By
7.9
we have proven above and
Item

E2
, for any
m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
, if follows that

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}(\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n},\Pi_{n}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))\right]}

\displaystyle\leq-\log\alpha_{n,m}+\inf_{\textit{Q}_{m}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n,m}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}\mathinner{\bigl(\textit{Q}_{m},\Pi_{n,m}\bigr)}+\textit{Q}_{m}\mathinner{\left[n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right]}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq-\log\alpha_{n,m}+\mathfrak{c}_{3}n(\eta_{n,m}+\zeta_{n,m})^{2}.

Thus, by
Item

E3
, for
m_{n}^{*}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
therein, it follows that

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\textup{KL}(\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n},\Pi_{n}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}))\right]}\lesssim n\epsilon_{n}^{2}+n(\eta_{n,m_{n}^{*}}+\zeta_{n,m_{n}^{*}})^{2}\lesssim n\epsilon_{n}^{2},

which completes the proof.

M.2.2
Proof of
Theorem

7.2

We first provide a technical lemma that plays a similar role to
Lemma

J.6
. Similarly to
J.4
, we define the event

\displaystyle\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural}(T,\bm{\Xi},\Pi,\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\mathbf{Y}^{(n)}\in\mathbbm{Y}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\int\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\textup{d}\Pi(\bm{\theta})\geq\exp\mathinner{\bigl(-T-\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi)\bigr)}\right\}}

(M.4)

for a parameter
\bm{\lambda}^{\star}\in\Lambda_{n}
, a positive value
T>0
and distributions
\bm{\Xi},\Pi\in\mathcal{P}(\Theta_{n,\mathcal{M}_{n}})
.

Lemma M.4
.

Assume that
7.7
holds. Then
for the event
\mathbbm{A}_{n}(T,\bm{\Xi},\Pi,\bm{\lambda}^{\star})
defined in
J.4
, we have

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}denom_{b}ound\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}quasi}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left(\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural}(T,\bm{\Xi},\Pi,\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right)}\geq 1-\frac{1}{\rho T}\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{c}_{2}\textit{Q}\mathinner{\left[n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right]}+1\right)}

(M.5)

Proof M.5
.

For notational simplicity, we denote the ratio of quasi-likelihoods by

\displaystyle\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}.

We start with applying
J.2
in
Lemma

J.4
with
\textsf{{F}}=\log\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})
,
\textit{Q}_{0}=\textit{Q}
and
\Pi_{0}=\Pi
to obtain

\displaystyle\log\int\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\textup{d}\Pi(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle\geq\int\log\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\bigr)}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\textup{d}\bm{\theta})-\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi).

Therefore, by applying Markov’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem and Jensen’s inequality in sequence, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left((\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural}(t,\bm{\Xi},\Pi,\bm{\lambda}^{\star}))^{\complement}\right)}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left(\log\int\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\textup{d}\Pi(\bm{\theta})\leq-T-\textup{KL}(\textit{Q},\Pi)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left(\int\log\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\bigr)}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\bm{\theta})\leq-T\right)}

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left(\int\log\mathinner{\bigl((\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta})))^{\rho}\bigr)}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\bm{\theta})\geq\rho T\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left(\int\log\mathinner{\bigl(1+\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}))\bigr)}^{\rho}\bigr)}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\bm{\theta})\geq\rho T\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho T}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left[\int\log\mathinner{\bigl(1+\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}))\bigr)}^{\rho}\bigr)}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\bm{\theta})\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho T}\int\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left[\log\mathinner{\left(1+\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}))\bigr)}^{\rho}\right)}\right]}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho T}\int\log\mathinner{\Bigl(1+\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{r}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}))\bigr)}^{\rho}\right]}\Bigr)}\textup{d}\textit{Q}(\bm{\theta})

By the assumption
7.7
and the inequality
\log(1+\e^z)\leq z+1
for
z>0
, the integrand in the last line of the above display is bounded by
