\displaystyle\log\mathinner{\left(1+\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right)}^{\rho}\right]}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathinner{\left(1+\textup{e}^{\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\mathfrak{c}_{2}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star}),

which completes the proof.

With
Lemma

M.4
in hand, we complete the proof as follows.

Proof M.6
(Proof of
Theorem

7.2
).

Fix
\bm{\lambda}_{n}^{\star}\in\Lambda_{n}^{\star}
and let

\mathcal{K}_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{n,\mathcal{M}_{n}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathscr{d}_{n}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\geq A_{n}\epsilon_{n}\right\}}.

Let
m^{*}_{n}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
be a model index that satisfies
Item

E3
and
\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}^{*}\in\mathcal{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}
be a distribution satisfying
Item

E2
. Now with the expression given in
M.4
, we define

\displaystyle\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural}\mathinner{\left(A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2},\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}^{*},\Pi_{n},\bm{\lambda}^{\star}\right)}.

Then we have by
Lemma

J.4
,

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}

\displaystyle[\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}^{\natural}(\mathcal{K}_{n})]\leq\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}((\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})^{\complement})+\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}[\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}^{\natural}(\mathcal{K}_{n})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})]

\displaystyle\leq\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}((\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})^{\complement})+\frac{1}{\upsilon_{n}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}[\textup{KL}(\widehat{\textit{Q}}_{n}^{\natural},\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\cdot|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})]+\frac{1}{\upsilon_{n}}\textup{e}^{\upsilon_{n}}\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}[\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\mathcal{K}_{n}|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})],

(M.6)

where
\upsilon_{n}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}
. We will show that each of the three terms in
M.6
goes to zero as
n\to\infty
. By
Lemma

M.4
together with
Item

E2
, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}((\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})^{\complement})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{c}_{2}\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}\mathinner{\left[n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})\right]}+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\rho A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{c}_{2}\mathfrak{c}_{3}(1+\mathfrak{c}_{4})^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}+1\right)}=\mathscr{o}(1)

since
A_{n}\to\infty
. The second term in
M.6
also converges to zero by employing
Theorem

7.1
with
Items

E2
and
E3
. Lastly, for the third term in
M.6
, we note that

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}[\Pi_{n}^{\natural}(\mathcal{K}_{n}|\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})]

\displaystyle=\textsf{{P}}^{(n)}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}\mathinner{\left[\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\textup{d}\Pi_{n}(\bm{\theta})}{\int\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\textup{d}\Pi_{n}(\bm{\theta})}\mathbbm{1}(\mathbbm{A}_{n}^{\natural})\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{A_{n}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}+\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}},\Pi_{n})}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\int_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\textup{d}\Pi_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]}.

(M.7)

By
7.6
in
Item

E1
, the expectation term of
M.7
is bounded by

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}quasi_{n}umer_{b}ound}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\int_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\textup{d}\Pi_{n}(\bm{\theta})\right]}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\lambda}^{\star}}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural}(\bm{\lambda}^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right]}\textup{d}\Pi_{n}(\bm{\theta})

(M.8)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}n\mathscr{d}_{n}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\bm{\theta}),\bm{\lambda}^{\star})}\textup{d}\Pi_{n}(\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle\leq\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{1}A_{n}^{2}n\epsilon_{n}^{2}}

for any sufficiently large
n
. In addition, by
Items

E2
and
E3
,

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}^{*},\Pi_{n})=-\log\alpha_{n,m_{n}^{*}}+\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{n,m_{n}^{*}}^{*},\Pi_{n,m_{n}^{*}})\leq(\mathfrak{c}_{5}+\mathfrak{c}_{3}(1+\mathfrak{c}_{4})^{2})n\epsilon_{n}^{2}.

Thus, since
A_{n}\to\infty
and
n\epsilon_{n}^{2}\geq 1
by assumption, the third term in
M.6
goes to zero. We complete the proof.

M.3
Proofs for
Section

I.1

M.3.1
Proof of
Lemma

I.1

Proof M.7
.

Let
\textsf{{P}}_{\Omega}
denote the expectation operator under the Bernoulli distribution with mean
\Omega\in[0,1]
. Note first that

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Omega}_{0}}^{(\bar{n})}\mathinner{\left[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{\bar{n}}^{\natural}(\bm{\Omega}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(\bar{n})})}{\textsf{{p}}_{\bar{n}}^{\natural}(\bm{\Omega}_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(\bar{n})})}\right]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\Omega_{0,i,j}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{-(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})^{2}+(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{-(\Omega_{1,i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})^{2}}\textsf{{P}}_{\Omega_{0,i,j}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{-2(\Omega_{0,i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})}\right]}\right\}}.

By Hoeffding’s lemma together with the fact that
\textsf{{P}}_{\texttt{{Bernoulli}}(\Omega_{0,i,j})}[-2(\Omega_{0,i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})]=0
, we further have

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\Omega_{0,i,j}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{-2(\Omega_{0,i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})}\right]}\leq\textup{e}^{\frac{1}{2}(\Omega_{0,i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})^{2}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Omega}_{0}}^{(\bar{n})}\mathinner{\left[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{\bar{n}}^{\natural}(\bm{\Omega}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(\bar{n})})}{\textsf{{p}}_{\bar{n}}^{\natural}(\bm{\Omega}_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(\bar{n})})}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\textup{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}(\Omega_{0,i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})^{2}}=\textup{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\bar{n}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\bm{\Omega}_{0},\bm{\Omega}_{1})},

which proves
I.2
. On the other hand, by the similar calculation

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\bm{\Omega}_{0}}^{(\bar{n})}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\left(\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{\bar{n}}^{\natural}(\bm{\Omega}_{0},\mathbf{Y}^{(\bar{n})})}{\textsf{{p}}_{\bar{n}}^{\natural}(\bm{\Omega}_{1},\mathbf{Y}^{(\bar{n})})}\right)}^{\rho}\right]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\textsf{{P}}_{\Omega_{0,i,j}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{-\rho(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})^{2}+\rho(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{\rho(\Omega_{1,i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})^{2}}\textsf{{P}}_{\Omega_{0,i,j}}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{2\rho(\Omega_{0,i,j}-\Omega_{1,i,j})(Y_{i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})}\right]}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{\rho(\Omega_{1,i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})^{2}}\textup{e}^{\frac{\rho^{2}}{2}(\Omega_{1,i,j}-\Omega_{0,i,j})^{2}}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\textup{e}^{\frac{\rho(\rho+2)}{2}\bar{n}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\bm{\Omega}_{0},\bm{\Omega}_{1})},

which proves
I.3
.

M.3.2
Proof of
Theorem

I.3

For the proof, the next lemma is useful.

Lemma M.8
.

Let
\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(U_{0,k,h})_{k\in[m],h\in[m]}\in\mathcal{U}_{m}
and
\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(U_{1,k,h})_{k\in[m],h\in[m]}\in\mathcal{U}_{m}
. Then for any
\mathbf{Z}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\mathbf{z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{z}_{n})^{\top}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(z_{ik})_{i\in[n],k\in[m]}\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,m}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{0},\mathbf{Z}),\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{Z}))\leq\max_{(k,h)\in[m]^{\otimes 2}}|U_{0,k,h}-U_{1,k,h}|.

Proof M.9
.

Let
\bar{n}_{k,h}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\mathbbm{1}(z_{ik}=1,z_{jh}=1)
. Then the conclusion straightforwardly follows from

\displaystyle\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{0},\mathbf{Z}),\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{Z}))

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n(n-2)}\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathbbm{L}_{n}}\mathinner{\left(\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{j}-\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n(n-2)}\sum_{(k,h)\in[m]^{\otimes 2}}\bar{n}_{k,h}(U_{0,k,h}-U_{1,k,h})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(k,h)\in[m]^{\otimes 2}}|U_{0,k,h}-U_{1,k,h}|^{2}.

The proof is done as follows.

Proof M.10
(Proof of
Theorem

I.3
).

Item

E1
is verified by
Lemma

I.1
. Thus it suffices to check
Item

E2
. Let

\displaystyle\eta_{\bar{n},m}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sup_{\bm{\Omega}^{*}\in\Lambda_{\bar{n}}^{\star}}\inf_{(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{Z})\in\Theta_{\bar{n},m}}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{Z}),\bm{\Omega}^{*})+n^{-1}

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bar{n},m}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sqrt{m^{2}\log n/n^{2}+\log m/n}.

By definition of infimum, for any
\bm{\Omega}^{\star}\in\Omega_{\bar{n}}^{\star}
, there exists
(\mathbf{U}_{\bar{n},m}^{*},\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m}^{*})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\mathbf{U}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}(\bm{\Omega}^{\star}),\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}(\bm{\Omega}^{\star}))\in\Theta_{\bar{n},m}
such that
\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{\bar{n},m}^{*},\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}),\bm{\Omega}^{\star})\leq\eta_{\bar{n},m}
. Moreover, let

\textit{Q}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\bigotimes_{(k,h)\in[m]^{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}k\leq h}\texttt{{Unif}}\mathinner{\left(\psi_{\bar{n},1,k,h}^{*},\psi_{\bar{n},2,k,h}^{*}\right)}\times\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\texttt{{Cat}}(\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m,i}^{*})\in\mathcal{Q}_{\bar{n},m},

where we denote
\psi_{\bar{n},1,k,h}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(U_{\bar{n},m,k,h}^{*}-m/n)\vee 0
and
\psi_{\bar{n},2,k,h}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(U_{\bar{n},m,k,h}^{*}+m/n)\wedge 1
and let
\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m,i}^{*}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
be the
i
-th row of
\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\textup{KL}(\textit{Q}_{\bar{n},m}^{*},\Pi_{\bar{n},m})

\displaystyle=\sum_{(k,h)\in[m]^{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}k\leq h}\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\texttt{{Unif}}\mathinner{\left(\psi_{\bar{n},1,k,h}^{*},\psi_{\bar{n},2,k,h}^{*}\right)},\texttt{{Unif}}(0,1)\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\textup{KL}\mathinner{\left(\texttt{{Cat}}(\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m,i}^{*}),\texttt{{Cat}}(m^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{n})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(k,h)\in[m]^{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}k\leq h}\log\mathinner{\left(\frac{2n}{m}\right)}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log m

\displaystyle\leq m^{2}\log n+n\log m\lesssim\bar{n}\zeta_{\bar{n},m}^{2}.

Moreover, by
Lemma

M.8
,

\displaystyle\textit{Q}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\left[\bar{n}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{Z}),\bm{\Omega}^{\star})\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\textit{Q}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\bar{n}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}\mathinner{\bigl(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{Z}),\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{\bar{n},m^{*}}^{*},\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m}^{*})\bigr)}\right]}+\bar{n}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}_{\bar{n},m}^{*},\mathbf{Z}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}),\bm{\Omega}^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq\bar{n}\textit{Q}_{\bar{n},m}^{*}\mathinner{\left[\mathinner{\Bigl(\max_{(k,h)\in[m]^{\otimes 2}}|U_{k,h}-U_{\bar{n},m,k,h}^{*}|\Bigr)}^{2}\right]}+\bar{n}\eta_{\bar{n},m^{*}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{n}\frac{(m)^{2}}{n^{2}}+\bar{n}\eta_{\bar{n},m^{*}}^{2}\leq\bar{n}(\zeta_{\bar{n},m^{*}}^{2}+\eta_{\bar{n},m^{*}}^{2}).

The proof is done.

M.3.3
Proof of
Corollary

I.6

For the proof, we employ the following approximation result for smooth graphons.

Lemma M.11
(Lemma 2.1 of
Gao et al. 2015
).

There exists an absolute constant
\mathfrak{c}_{1}>0
such that for any
\bm{\Omega}^{\star}\in\Lambda_{n}^{\textup{{G}}}\mathinner{(\mathcal{H}^{\beta,2,F_{0}}_{[0,1]})}
and
m\in[n]
,

\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U}^{*},\mathbf{Z}^{*}),\bm{\Omega}^{\star})\leq\mathfrak{c}_{1}F_{0}^{2}\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{m^{2}}\right)}^{\beta\wedge 1}

for some
\mathbf{U}^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{m}
and
\mathbf{Z}^{*}\in\mathcal{Z}_{n,m}
.

Proof M.12
(Proof of
Corollary

I.6
).

Let
\Lambda_{\bar{n}}^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Lambda_{\bar{n}}^{\textup{{G}}}\mathinner{(\mathcal{H}^{\beta,2,F_{0}}_{[0,1]})}
for simplicity. Let
k_{n}^{*}\in\mathinner{[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\lceil\frac{1}{2}\log n\rceil]}
be an integer such that

\left\lfloor{n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{\frac{1}{\beta\wedge 1+1}}\leq\left\lfloor{n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n}}\right\rfloor\leq n^{(k_{n}^{*}+1)/\log n}

and let
m_{n}^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lfloor n^{k_{n}^{*}/\log n}\rfloor
. Note that
n^{\frac{1}{\beta\wedge 1+1}}\geq m_{n}^{*}\gtrsim n^{-1/\log n}n^{\frac{1}{\beta\wedge 1+1}}=\textup{e}^{-1}n^{\frac{1}{\beta\wedge 1+1}}
. Therefore, by
Lemma

M.11
,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}^{2}(\Lambda_{\bar{n}}^{\star})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\bm{\Omega}^{*}\in\Lambda_{\bar{n}}^{\star}}\inf_{(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{Z})\in\Theta_{\bar{n},m_{n}^{*}}}\mathscr{d}_{\bar{n},2}^{2}(\textsf{{T}}(\mathbf{U},\mathbf{Z}),\bm{\Omega}^{*})+\mathinner{\left(\frac{(m_{n}^{*})^{2}}{n^{2}}\log n+\frac{\log m_{n}^{*}}{n}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathinner{\left(\frac{1}{(m_{n}^{*})^{2}}\right)}^{\beta\wedge 1}+n^{\frac{2}{\beta\wedge 1+1}-2}\log n+\frac{\log n}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\frac{2(\beta\wedge 1)}{\beta\wedge 1+1}}\log n=n^{-\mathinner{\left(\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}\right)}\wedge 1}\log n,

which completes the proof.

M.4
Proof for
Section

I.2

M.4.1
Proof of
Lemma

I.8

Proof M.13
.

Let
\textsf{{P}}_{\star,i}
be the marginal distribution of
Y_{i}
under
\textsf{{P}}_{\star}^{(n)}
for
i\in[n]
. Since
Y_{i}
is sub-Gaussian with mean
f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i})
and variance proxy
\varsigma^{2}>0
,
I.11
follows from

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\star}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural,\kappa}(f,\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural,\kappa}(f^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\textsf{{P}}_{\star,i}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{-\kappa(f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}))(Y_{i}-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))}\right]}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\textup{e}^{(\kappa^{2}\varsigma^{2}/2)(f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textup{e}^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}(1-\kappa\varsigma^{2})(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}.

Similarly,
I.12
follows from

\displaystyle\textsf{{P}}_{\star}^{(n)}\mathinner{\left[\frac{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural,\kappa}(f^{\star},\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}{\textsf{{p}}_{n}^{\natural,\kappa}(f,\mathbf{Y}^{(n)})}\right]}^{\rho}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathinner{\left\{\textup{e}^{\frac{\rho\kappa}{2}(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}\textsf{{P}}_{\star,i}\mathinner{\left[\textup{e}^{\rho\kappa(f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i}))(Y_{i}-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))}\right]}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textup{e}^{\frac{1}{2}\rho\kappa(1+\rho\kappa\varsigma^{2})(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f^{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{i}))^{2}}.

The proof is done.

Appendix N
Proofs of neural network approximation results in
Section

K.1.1

N.1
Approximation of Hölder smooth functions

In this subsection, we provide the proof of
Theorem

K.1
as well as additional lemmas used in the proof.

N.1.1
Additional notation

We first introduce some additional definitions and notation. For
T\in\mathbbm{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1/(3T)]
and
d\in\mathbbm{N}
, we define

\displaystyle{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}={{\boxplus}}(T,\delta,d)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x_{i}\notin\bigcup_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathinner{\left(\frac{t}{T}-\delta,\frac{t}{T}\right)}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\mathbf{t}\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T-1]^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}\mathinner{\left[\frac{t_{j}}{T},\frac{t_{j}+1}{T}-\delta\mathbbm{1}(t_{j}\neq T-1)\right]},

which is an union of “
\delta
-margined sub-hypercubes” that are of the form
\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}[t_{j}/T,(t_{j}+1)/T-\delta\mathbbm{1}(t_{j}\neq T-1)]\subset[0,1]^{d}
. Note that
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)\subsetneq[0,1]^{d}
, and the region
[0,1]^{d}\setminus{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
will not be considered in our first approximating neural network construction. Moreover, for
\mathbf{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(x_{j})_{j\in[d]}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
, let

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}grid_{a}llocation}\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathfrak{v}_{T,\delta,d}(\mathbf{x})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left(\frac{\left\lfloor{Tx_{j}}\right\rfloor}{T}-\frac{T-1}{T}\mathbbm{1}(x_{j}=1)\right)}_{j\in[d]},

(N.1)

which is the leftmost vertex of the
\delta
-margined sub-hypercube, to which the input
\mathbf{x}
belongs. For
\beta>0
and
d\in\mathbbm{N}
, we denote

\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left\{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{a}|_{1}<\beta\right\}},

which can be viewed as a set of
d
-dimensional monomial exponents with degree less than
\beta>0
. Lastly, for a continuous function
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,d}
, its modulus of continuity
\beth_{f}
is a function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
defined as

\beth_{f}(\delta)=\sup_{\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2}\in[0,1]^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{x}_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{2}|_{2}\leq\delta}|f(\mathbf{x}_{1})-f(\mathbf{x}_{2})|

for
\delta>0
. Note that since
|\mathbf{x}_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{2}|_{\infty}\leq|\mathbf{x}_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{2}|_{2}
for any
\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have that
\beth_{f}(\delta)\leq F_{0}\delta^{\beta\wedge 1}
for any
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}
.

N.1.2
Proof of
Theorem

K.1

We first provide two key lemmas and then give the proof of
Theorem

K.1
.

Lemma N.1
.

Suppose that given
\varepsilon>0
,
T\in\mathbbm{N}
,
\delta\in(0,1/(3T)]
and
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,d}
, there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\Theta_{(K^{\dagger},M^{\dagger})}^{\leq B^{\dagger}}
for
K^{\dagger}\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
,
M^{\dagger}\in\mathbbm{N}
and
B^{\dagger}>0
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{0})(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}\leq\varepsilon.

Then, there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{(K,M)}^{\leq B}
with
K\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=K^{\dagger}+4d
,
M\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=3^{d}(M^{\dagger}\vee 14)
and
B\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(B^{\dagger}\vee 1)+d\delta
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}\leq\varepsilon+d\beth_{f}(\delta).

Proof N.2
.

The proof is deferred to
Section

N.1.4
.

Lemma N.3
.

Let
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}
,
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
,
M\in\mathbbm{N}
and
\delta\in(0,1/(3T)]
with
T\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lfloor M^{1/d}\rfloor^{2}
. Then there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{(K^{\dagger},M^{\dagger})}^{\leq B^{\dagger}}
with

\displaystyle K^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(K+12)+(K+5)\left\lceil{\log_{2}(\beta\vee 1)}\right\rceil,

\displaystyle M^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=4dM+2\max\mathinner{\left\{2dM^{1/d},3\left\lceil{\beta}\right\rceil(d+1)^{\lceil\beta\rceil}\right\}},

\displaystyle B^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max\mathinner{\left\{M+2,2(\delta M^{2/d})^{-1},F_{0}\right\}}

such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}\leq(3\beta^{2}+2)F_{0}(d+1)^{\lceil\beta\rceil}2^{-K}+C_{\beta,d,F_{0}}M^{-\beta/d},

where
C_{\beta,d,F_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=F_{0}\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{a}|_{1}=\left\lceil{\beta-1}\right\rceil}(1/\mathbf{a}!)
.

Proof N.4
.

The proof is deferred to
Section

N.1.5
.

Theorem

K.1
is immediate from
Lemmas

N.1
and
N.3
.

Proof N.5
(Proof of
Theorem

K.1
).

Recall that
\beth_{f}(\delta)\leq F_{0}\delta^{\beta\wedge 1}
for any
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}
. Thus taking
\delta=(6M^{2/d})^{-\beta/(\beta\wedge 1)}\leq(3T)^{-1}
, we obtain the approximation error in the conclusion by
Lemmas

N.1
and
N.3
. To specify the magnitude bound
B
, we note that

\displaystyle M\vee(\delta M^{2/d})^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 6^{\beta/(\beta\wedge 1)}\mathinner{\left\{M\vee(M^{2/d})^{\beta/(\beta\wedge 1)-1}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq 6^{\beta/(\beta\wedge 1)}\mathinner{\left\{M\vee(M^{2(\beta-1)/d})\right\}}.

We complete the proof.

N.1.3
Basic lemmas

In this subsection, we give basic technical lemmas related to neural network construction, which are frequently used in the proofs of the lemmas in this section.

Lemma N.6
.

Let
K_{1},K_{2}\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
and let
\mathbf{M}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(M_{1,1},\dots,M_{1,K_{1}+1})\in\mathbbm{N}^{K_{1}+1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(M_{2,1},\dots,M_{2,K_{2}+1})\in\mathbbm{N}^{K_{2}+1}
with
M_{1,K_{1}+1}=M_{2,1}
. Then for any two network parameters
\bm{\theta}_{1}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{\mathbf{M}_{1}}
and
\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{\mathbf{M}_{2}}
, there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{(K,M)}^{\leq B}
with
K\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=K_{1}+K_{2}
,
M\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=|\mathbf{M}_{1}|_{\infty}\vee|\mathbf{M}_{2}|_{\infty}\vee(2M_{1,K_{1}+1})
and
B\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=|\bm{\theta}_{1}|_{\infty}\vee|\bm{\theta}_{2}|_{\infty}
such that
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})\equiv\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2})\circ\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})
.

Proof N.7
.

We write
\bm{\theta}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=((\mathbf{W}_{1,k},\mathbf{b}_{1,k}))_{k\in[K_{1}]}
and
\bm{\theta}_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=((\mathbf{W}_{2,k},\mathbf{b}_{2,k}))_{k\in[K_{2}]}
for convenience. We construct
\bm{\theta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=((\mathbf{W}_{k},\mathbf{b}_{k}))_{k\in[K]}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Big((\mathbf{W}_{1,1}

\displaystyle,\mathbf{b}_{1,1}),\dots,(\mathbf{W}_{1,K_{1}-1},\mathbf{b}_{1,K_{1}-1}),(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,K_{1}},\mathbf{b}_{1,K_{1}}^{*}),

\displaystyle(\mathbf{W}^{*}_{2,1},\mathbf{b}_{2,1}),(\mathbf{W}_{2,2},\mathbf{b}_{2,2}),\dots,(\mathbf{W}_{2,K_{2}},\mathbf{b}_{2,K_{2}})\Big),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{*}_{1,K_{1}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{W}_{1,K_{1}}\\
-\mathbf{W}_{1,K_{1}-1}\end{array}\right)}\quad\mathbf{b}^{*}_{1,K_{1}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{b}_{1,K_{1}}\\
-\mathbf{b}_{1,K_{1}-1}\end{array}\right)}\quad\mathbf{W}^{*}_{2,1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{W}_{2,1}&-\mathbf{W}_{2,1}\end{array}\right)}.

Then the output of the
K_{1}
-th hidden layer of
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})
is equal to
(\mathrm{ReLU}(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})),\mathrm{ReLU}(-\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})))
. Thus by the identity
x=\mathrm{ReLU}(x)-\mathrm{ReLU}(-x)
, we obtain the desired result.

Lemma N.8
.

For any
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
, there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\textup{id},K}\in\Theta_{(K,2)}^{\leq 1}
such that

\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\textup{id},K})(x)=x

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Proof N.9
.

By the identity
x=\mathrm{ReLU}(x)-\mathrm{ReLU}(-x)
and the projection property
\mathrm{ReLU}\circ\mathrm{ReLU}\equiv\mathrm{ReLU}
of the ReLU activation function, the desired result easily follows.

N.1.4
Proof of
Lemma

N.1

We first state two key lemmas, which are taken from the existing literature.

Lemma N.10
(Lemma 3.1 of
Lu et al. 2021
, Lemma 6.2 of
Yang et al. 2022
).

There exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mid}}\in\Theta_{(4,14)}^{\leq 1}
such that

\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mid}})(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=\mathrm{mid}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

for any
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
\mathrm{mid}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}^{3}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is a
function returning the middle value of three inputs.

Proof N.11
.

Note that we have a neural network expression for the max function such as

\displaystyle\max(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(x_{1}+x_{2})+\frac{1}{2}|x_{1}-x_{2}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{ReLU}(x_{1}+x_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{ReLU}(-x_{1}-x_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{ReLU}(x_{1}-x_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{ReLU}(-x_{1}+x_{2})

\displaystyle=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\max,2})(x_{1},x_{2})\mbox{ with }\bm{\theta}_{\max,2}\in\Theta_{(2,4)}^{\leq 1}.

Thus, using
Lemma

N.6
, we get a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\max,3}\in\Theta_{(4,6)}^{\leq 1}
such that

\displaystyle\max(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=\max\mathinner{\left(\max(x_{1},x_{2}),\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{id},2})(x_{3})\right)}=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\max,3})(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

for any
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{id},2}\in\Theta_{(2,2)}^{\leq 1}
is the network parameter constructed in
Lemma

N.8
, whose realization is the identity on
\mathbb{R}
. Similarly, we can find a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\min,3}\in\Theta_{(4,6)}^{\leq 1}
such that
\min(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\equiv\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\min,3})(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
. By the above constructions,
Lemma

N.8
and the fact that

\displaystyle\mathrm{mid}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})

\displaystyle=x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}-\max(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})-\min(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}),

we conclude the desired result.

Lemma N.12
(Lemma 3.4 of
Lu et al. 2021
).

Suppose that, given
\varepsilon>0
,
T\in\mathbbm{N}
and
\delta\in(0,1/(3T)]
, two functions
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,d}
and
g_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
satisfy

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert g_{0}(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}\leq\varepsilon.

Then if we recursively define functions
g_{1},\dots,g_{d}
as

g_{j}(\cdot)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathrm{mid}\mathinner{\left(g_{j-1}(\cdot-\delta\mathbf{e}_{j}),g_{j-1}(\cdot),g_{j-1}(\cdot+\delta\mathbf{e}_{j})\right)}

for
j=[d]
, it follows that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert g_{d}(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}\leq\varepsilon+d\beth_{f}(\delta),

where
\{\mathbf{e}_{j}\}_{j\in[d]}
is the standard basis in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

We now provide the proof of
Lemma

N.1
.

Proof N.13
(Proof of
Lemma

N.1
).

We will prove the desired result by employing
Lemma

N.12
in with
g_{0}=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{0})
. By induction, it suffices to show that if
g_{j-1}=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{j-1})
with
\bm{\theta}_{j-1}\in\Theta_{(K_{j},M_{j})}^{\leq B_{j}}
,

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}mid_{i}nduction}g_{j}=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{j})\mbox{ for some }\bm{\theta}_{j}\in\Theta_{(K_{j}+4,3M_{j}\vee 14)}^{\leq(B_{j}+\delta)\vee 1}.

(N.2)

By just adding
-\delta\mathbf{e}_{j}
or
\delta\mathbf{e}_{j}
to the first bias vector of
\bm{\theta}_{j-1}
, we can easily construct two network parameters
\bm{\theta}_{j-1}^{-}\in\Theta_{(K_{j},M_{j})}^{\leq B_{j}+\delta}
and
\bm{\theta}_{j-1}^{+}\in\Theta_{(K_{j},M_{j})}^{\leq B_{j}+\delta}
such that
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{j-1}^{-})(\cdot)\equiv g_{j-1}(\cdot-\delta\mathbf{e}_{j})
and
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{j-1}^{+})(\cdot)\equiv g_{j-1}(\cdot+\delta\mathbf{e}_{j})
. On the other hand, by
Lemma

N.10
, there is a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mid}}\in\Theta_{(4,14)}^{\leq 1}
that exactly recovers the middle value function. Therefore, using
Lemma

N.6
, we prove
N.2
.

N.1.5
Proof of
Lemma

N.3

Before the proof, we give five lemmas used in the proof. First, we borrow the following two lemmas from
Schmidt-Hieber 2020
.

Lemma N.14
(Lemma A.2 of
Schmidt-Hieber 2020
).

For any
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
, there exists a network parameter

\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{\mathbf{M}}\mbox{ with }\mathbf{M}=\mathinner{\left(2,6,\dots,6,1\right)}\in\mathbbm{N}^{6+K}

such that

\sup_{(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2})\in[0,1]^{2}}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K})(x_{1},x_{2})-x_{1}x_{2}\right\rvert}\leq 2^{-K}

and
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K})(x_{1},0)=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K})(0,x_{2})=0
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in[0,1]
.

Lemma N.15
(Lemma A.4 of
Schmidt-Hieber 2020
).

For any
d\in\mathbbm{N}
,
\beta>0
and
K\in\mathbbm{N}_{\geq 2}
, there exists a network parameter

\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mon},d,\beta,K}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{\mathbf{M}}\mbox{ with }\mathbf{M}=\mathinner{\left(d,6\left\lceil{\beta}\right\rceil|\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}|,\dots,6\left\lceil{\beta}\right\rceil|\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}|,|\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}|\right)}\in\mathbbm{N}^{3+(K+5)\left\lceil{\log_{2}(\beta\vee 1)}\right\rceil}

such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{d}}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mon},d,\beta,K})(\mathbf{x})-(\mathbf{x}^{\mathbf{a}})_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\right\rvert}_{\infty}\leq\beta^{2}2^{-K}

and
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mon},d,\beta,K})\in[0,1]^{|\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}|}
.

The next lemma introduces a neural network that appropriately “discretizes” an one-dimensional input. This neural network is useful to approximate the function
\mathfrak{v}(\cdot)
defined in
N.1
.

Lemma N.16
.

Let
T_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
,
T\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=T_{0}^{2}
and
\delta\in(0,1/(3T)]
. Then there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{(1,T_{0},2,T_{0},1)}
such that

\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta})(x)=\frac{\left\lfloor{Tx}\right\rfloor}{T}-\frac{T-1}{T}\mathbbm{1}(x=1)

for any
x\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta,1)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=[0,1]\setminus(\cup_{t=1}^{T-1}(t/T-\delta,t/T))
and
|\bm{\theta}|_{\infty}\leq(\delta T)^{-1}

Proof N.17
.

Let
\mathrm{step}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[0,1]\mapsto[0,1]
be a function defined as

\mathrm{step}(x)=\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1}{\delta T}\mathinner{\left(\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-\frac{t}{T}+\delta\right)}-\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-\frac{t}{T}\right)}\right)}

for
x\in[0,1]
. Then we have that
\mathrm{step}(x)=\left\lfloor{Tx}\right\rfloor/T-\{(T-1)/T\}\mathbbm{1}(x=1)
for any
x\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta,1)
. Thus, it remains to construct a neural network that recovers the function
\mathrm{step}(\cdot)
.

Let
\bm{\theta}_{1}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{(1,2T_{0}-2,1)}
and
\bm{\theta}_{2}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{(1,2T_{0}-2,1)}
be network parameters such that

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}-1}\frac{T_{0}}{\delta T}\mathinner{\left(\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t\frac{T_{0}}{T}+\delta\right)}-\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t\frac{T_{0}}{T}\right)}\right)},

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2})(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}-1}\frac{1}{\delta T}\mathinner{\left(\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-\frac{t}{T}+\delta\right)}-\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-\frac{t}{T}\right)}\right)}

for
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Then for any
x\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta,1)
, we have
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(x)=\left\lfloor{T_{0}x}\right\rfloor/T_{0}-\{(T_{0}-1)/T_{0}\}\mathbbm{1}(x=1)
. Since

x-\frac{\left\lfloor{T_{0}x}\right\rfloor}{T_{0}}\in\bigcup_{t=0}^{T_{0}-1}\mathinner{\left[\frac{t}{T},\frac{t+1}{T}-\delta\right]}\subset{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta,1)

for any
x\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta,1)\setminus\{1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})+

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2})(x-\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(x))

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\lfloor{T_{0}x}\right\rfloor}{T_{0}}-\frac{T_{0}-1}{T_{0}}\mathbbm{1}(x=1)+\frac{\left\lfloor{T(x-\left\lfloor{T_{0}x}\right\rfloor/T_{0})}\right\rfloor}{T}+\frac{T_{0}-1}{T}\mathbbm{1}(x=1)

\displaystyle=\frac{T_{0}\left\lfloor{T_{0}x}\right\rfloor}{T}+\frac{\left\lfloor{Tx-T_{0}\left\lfloor{T_{0}x}\right\rfloor}\right\rfloor}{T}+\frac{T-1}{T}\mathbbm{1}(x=1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\lfloor{Tx}\right\rfloor}{T}+\frac{T-1}{T}\mathbbm{1}(x=1).

Thus, using the identity
\mathrm{ReLU}(x)=x
for
x\geq 0
, a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta}
such that
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta})\equiv\mathrm{ReLU}(\mathrm{ReLU}(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})))+\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2})(\mathrm{ReLU}(\mathrm{ReLU}(x)-\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(x)))
satisfies the desired properties in the lemma.

The following lemma constructs a neural network enumerates the mutivariate indices
\mathbf{t}\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(T-1)]^{d}
.

Lemma N.18
.

Let
d\in\mathbbm{N}
,
T_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
and
T\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=T_{0}^{2}
. Then, there is a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{1,2d(T_{0}+1),2d,2}
such that

\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}})(\mathbf{t})=\mathinner{\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\lfloor{\frac{t_{j}}{T_{0}}}\right\rfloor T_{0}^{j-1},1+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathinner{\left(t_{j}-T_{0}\left\lfloor{\frac{t_{j}}{T_{0}}}\right\rfloor\right)}T_{0}^{j-1}\right)}

for any
\mathbf{t}\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(T-1)]^{d}
and
|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}}|_{\infty}\leq T_{0}^{d-1}
.

Proof N.19
.

Let
\bm{\theta}_{1}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{1,2T_{0},1}
be a network parameter such that

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(x)=\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{T_{0}}2\mathinner{\left\{\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t^{\prime}T_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\right)}-\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t^{\prime}T_{0}\right)}\right\}},

for
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, for any
t\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(T-1)]
, we have
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(t)=\left\lfloor{t/T_{0}}\right\rfloor
, and thus

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2,j})(t)

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\left(T_{0}^{j-1}\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(t),T_{0}^{j-1}(\mathrm{ReLU}(t)-T_{0}\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})(t))\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\left(\left\lfloor{\frac{t_{j}}{T_{0}}}\right\rfloor T_{0}^{j-1},\mathinner{\left(t_{j}-T_{0}\left\lfloor{\frac{t_{j}}{T_{0}}}\right\rfloor\right)}T_{0}^{j-1}\right)}

for each
j\in[d]
, where
\bm{\theta}_{2,j}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{1,2T_{0}+1,2}
is a neural network with
|\bm{\theta}_{2,j}|_{\infty}\leq T_{0}^{(j-1)\vee 2}
. Since
\mathrm{ReLU}(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2,j})(t))=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2,j})(t)
for any
t\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(T-1)]
, the neural network
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{1,2d(T_{0}+1),2d,2}
defined as

\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}})\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathrm{ReLU}(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2,j}))+\mathbf{1}_{2}

satisfies the desired condition of the lemma.

Lastly, we construct the following “point-fitting” neural network that approximates the local Taylor coefficients
\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f(\mathbf{t}/T)
for
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}
and
\mathbf{t}\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T-1]^{d}
.

Lemma N.20
.

Let
T_{0}\in\mathbbm{N}
and
\mathbf{Y}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(y_{t_{1},t_{2}})_{t_{1}\in[T_{0}],t_{2}\in[T_{0}]}\in[0,1]^{T_{0}\times T_{0}}
. Then there exists a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{Y},T_{0}}\in\Theta_{(6,4T_{0})}^{\leq B}
with
B\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max\mathinner{\left\{3,T_{0}+1/6\right\}}
such that

\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{Y},T_{0}})(t_{1},t_{2})=y_{t_{1},t_{2}}

for any
(t_{1},t_{2})\in[T_{0}]^{\otimes 2}
.

Proof N.21
.

For
T\in\mathbbm{N}
, let
\xi_{\mathrm{id},T}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{id},T})
, where
\bm{\theta}_{\textup{id},T}\in\Theta_{(T,2)}^{\leq 1}
is the depth
T
network parameter constructed in
Lemma

N.8
, whose realization is the identity on
\mathbb{R}
. For
t\in\mathbbm{N}
, define a function
\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\mapsto[0,1]
as

\displaystyle\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}(x)=\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(1-3\mathinner{\left\{\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t-\frac{1}{6}\right)}+\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(-x+t-\frac{1}{6}\right)}\right\}}\right)}

for
x\in\mathbb{R}.
Note that
\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}(x)=1
if
x\in[t-1/6,t+1/6]
,
\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}(x)=0
if
x\in(-\infty,t-1/2]\cup[t+1/2,\infty)
and
0\leq\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}\leq 1
on
\mathbb{R}
. For notational convenience, let
\xi_{\mathrm{hat},1\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t})_{t\in[T_{0}]}\in[0,1]^{T_{0}}
. We also define a function
\xi_{\mathrm{test},t}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\mapsto[0,1]
as

\displaystyle\xi_{\mathrm{test},t}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(\xi_{\mathrm{id},2}(y)-3\mathinner{\left\{\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t-\frac{1}{6}\right)}+\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(-x+t-\frac{1}{6}\right)}\right\}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(\mathrm{ReLU}(y)-\mathrm{ReLU}(-y)-3\mathinner{\left\{\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(x-t-\frac{1}{6}\right)}+\mathrm{ReLU}\mathinner{\left(-x+t-\frac{1}{6}\right)}\right\}}\right)}

for
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
. Then, for any
y\in[0,1]
, we have
\xi_{\mathrm{test},t}(x,y)=y
for
x\in[t-1/6,t+1/6]
and
\xi_{\mathrm{test},t}(x,y)=0
for
x\in(-\infty,t-1/2]\cup[t+1/2,\infty)
.

Next, recall
\mathbf{Y}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(y_{t_{1},t_{2}})_{t_{1}\in[T_{0}],t_{2}\in[T_{0}]}\in[0,1]^{T_{0}\times T_{0}}
. Note that, by the assumption that every
y_{t_{1},t_{2}}
lies in
[0,1]
and the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}}\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}(x)\in[0,1]
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathbf{e}_{T_{0},t}^{\top}\mathrm{ReLU}(\mathbf{Y}\xi_{\mathrm{hat},1\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T_{0}}(x))\in[0,1]
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
and any
t\in[T_{0}]
, where
\mathbf{e}_{T_{0},t}\in\mathbb{R}^{T_{1}}
is the
T_{0}
-dimensional vector with a 1 in the
t
-th coordinate and 0’s elsewhere. Then, since both
\xi_{\mathrm{hat},t}
and
\xi_{\mathrm{test},t}
can be exactly represented by neural networks with depth 3, there is a depth 6 neural network
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{Y},T_{0}}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{(2,2T_{0}+2,T_{0}+2,T_{0}+1,4T_{0},T_{0},1)}
such that

\displaystyle\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{Y},T_{0}})(x_{1},x_{2})=\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}}\xi_{\mathrm{test},t}\mathinner{\left(\mathrm{ReLU}(\xi_{\mathrm{id},3}(x_{2})),\mathbf{e}_{T_{0},i}^{\top}\mathrm{ReLU}(\mathbf{Y}\xi_{\mathrm{hat},1\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T_{0}}(x_{1}))\right)}

for
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.
Then it is easy to see that
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{Y},T_{0}})(t_{1},t_{2})=y_{t_{1},t_{2}}
for any
(t_{1},t_{2})\in[T_{0}]^{\otimes 2}
and that
|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{Y},T_{0}}|_{\infty}\leq\max\mathinner{\left\{3,T_{0}+1/6\right\}}
. We complete the proof.

We are ready to prove
Lemma

N.3
.

Proof N.22
(Proof of
Lemma

N.3
).

By Taylor’s theorem, the assumption that
\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d}}\leq F_{0}
and the inequality that
|(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}|\leq|\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})|_{\infty}^{|\mathbf{a}|_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}app_{e}rr_{t}aylor}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lvert f(\mathbf{x})-\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\frac{\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))}{\mathbf{a}!}(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}

(N.3)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{a}|_{1}=\left\lceil{\beta-1}\right\rceil}\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\mathinner{\left[\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})+u(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})))-\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))\right]}\frac{(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}}{\mathbf{a}!}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{a}|_{1}=\left\lceil{\beta-1}\right\rceil}\mathinner{\left(F_{0}\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert u(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))\right\rvert}_{\infty}^{\beta-\left\lceil{\beta-1}\right\rceil}\frac{|\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})|_{\infty}^{|\mathbf{a}|_{1}}}{\mathbf{a}!}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\left(F_{0}\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{a}|_{1}=\left\lceil{\beta-1}\right\rceil}\frac{1}{\mathbf{a}!}\right)}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}|\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})|_{\infty}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,d,F_{0}}T^{-\beta},

where we used multi-index notation
\mathbf{x}^{\mathbf{a}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\prod_{j=1}^{d}x_{j}^{a_{j}}
and
\mathbf{a}!\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\prod_{j=1}^{d}(a_{j}!)
and let
C_{\beta,d,F_{0}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=F_{0}\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbbm{N}_{0}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}|\mathbf{a}|_{1}=\left\lceil{\beta-1}\right\rceil}(1/\mathbf{a}!)
. Thus it suffices to find a neural network approximation of the local Taylor polynomial function

P_{f}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\frac{\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))}{\mathbf{a}!}(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}

(N.4)

supported on
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
.

Let
T_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\lfloor M^{1/d}\rfloor=\sqrt{T}
. For
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta}\in\Theta_{(4,T_{0}\vee 2)}^{\leq(\delta T)^{-1}}
defined in
Lemma

N.16
, we let
\xi_{\mathrm{grid}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)\mapsto[0,1]^{d}
be a function defined as

\xi_{\mathrm{grid}}(\mathbf{x})=\mathinner{\left(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta})(x_{j})\right)}_{j\in[d]}

for
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
. Note that
\xi_{\mathrm{grid}}
is a neural network with depth
4
and width
d(T_{0}\vee 2)\leq 2dT_{0}
, which satisfies
\xi_{\mathrm{grid}}\equiv\mathfrak{v}
on
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
. We also define a function
\xi_{\mathrm{mon}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}})_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}|}
such that

\xi_{\mathrm{mon}}(\mathbf{x})=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mon},d,\beta,K})(\mathbf{x}-\xi_{\mathrm{grid}}(\mathbf{x}))

for
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
, where
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mon},d,\beta,K}
is a network parameter with depth
2+(K+5)\lceil\log_{2}(\beta\vee 1)\rceil
and width
6\lceil\beta\rceil|\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}|\leq 6\lceil\beta\rceil(d+1)^{\lceil\beta\rceil}
, which is defined in
Lemma

N.15
. Then by
Lemma

N.15
, we have that

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}app_{e}rr_{m}om}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})-(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}

(N.5)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mon},d,\beta,K})(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))-(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}\leq\beta^{2}2^{-K}

for any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}
.
On the other hand, since the identity map
\mathbf{x}\mapsto\mathbf{x}
can be exactly represented by a neural network with width
2d
by
Lemma

N.8
,
Lemma

N.6
implies that there is a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{1}
with depth
K_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=6+(K+5)\left\lceil{\log_{2}(\beta\vee 1)}\right\rceil
and width
M_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(4dM^{1/d})\vee(6\lceil\beta\rceil(d+1)^{\lceil\beta\rceil})\geq(2d(T_{0}+1))\vee(6\lceil\beta\rceil(d+1)^{\lceil\beta\rceil})
, which satisfies
\xi_{\mathrm{mon}}\equiv\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1})
on
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
.

We then for each
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}
construct a neural network that approximates the local Taylor coefficients. First let
\xi_{\mathrm{enum}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T-1]^{d}\mapsto[T_{0}^{d}]^{\otimes 2}
be a function such that

\xi_{\mathrm{enum}}(\mathbf{t})=\mathinner{\left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left\lfloor{\frac{t_{j}}{T_{0}}}\right\rfloor T_{0}^{j-1},1+\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathinner{\left(t_{j}-T_{0}\left\lfloor{\frac{t_{j}}{T_{0}}}\right\rfloor\right)}T_{0}^{j-1}\right)}.

Note that the map
\xi_{\mathrm{enum}}
is a bijection and can be exactly represented by a neural network
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}}\in\widehat{\Theta}_{1,2d(T_{0}+1),2d,1}
with
|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}}|_{\infty}\leq T_{0}^{d-1}
by
Lemma

N.18
. On the other hand, for
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}
and
\mathbf{t}\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T-1]^{d}
, let

h_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{t}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathinner{\left(F_{0}^{-1}\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f\mathinner{(T^{-1}\mathbf{t})}+1\right)}

and
\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(h_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{t}})_{\mathbf{t}\in[0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}T-1]^{d}}
. With this notation, we can write

P_{f}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\frac{F_{0}}{\mathbf{a}!}\mathinner{\left(2\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}-1\right)}(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}

for any
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
. We use
Lemma

N.20
to complete the construction. Since
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,d,F_{0}}
, we have
|\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}}|_{\infty}\leq 1
, and so we can apply the lemma. Let
\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)\mapsto\mathbb{R}
be a function defined as

\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}},T_{0}^{d}})\circ\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}})\circ\mathinner{\bigl(T\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta})(\mathbf{x})\bigr)}

for
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
, where
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}},T_{0}^{d}}
is a network parameter with depth
6
and width
4T_{0}^{d}
, which satisfies

\textup{{net}}\mathinner{\bigl(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}},T_{0}^{d}}\bigr)}\circ\xi_{\mathrm{enum}}(\mathbf{t})=h_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{t}}

and
|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}},T_{0}^{d}}|_{\infty}\leq T_{0}^{d}+2\leq M+2
. The existence of the network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}},T_{0}^{d}}
is guaranteed by
Lemma

N.20
. Then since
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}})\circ(T\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{step},T_{0},\delta}))\equiv\xi_{\mathrm{enum}}\circ\mathfrak{v}
on
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
, we have

\displaystyle\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}app_{e}rr_{c}oef}

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathinner{\left(2F_{0}\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})-F_{0}\right)}-\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})\right)}\right\rvert}

(N.6)

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\mathinner{\left(2F_{0}\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{fit},\mathbf{h}_{\mathbf{a}},T_{0}^{d}})\circ\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{enum},T_{0}})(T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))-F_{0}\right)}-\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})\right)}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\left\lvert 2F_{0}h_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{t}}-F_{0}-\partial^{\mathbf{a}}f\mathinner{\left(\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})\right)}\right\rvert}=0

for any
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
. Due to
Lemma

N.6
, there is a neural network
\bm{\theta}_{2,\mathbf{a}}\in\Theta_{(K_{2},M_{2})}^{\leq B_{2}}
with
K_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=13=6+4+3
,
M_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=4dM\geq\max\{2d(T_{0}+1),4T_{0}^{d}\}
and
B_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max\{M+2,2(\delta M^{2/d})^{-1}\}
(since
\delta T=\delta\lfloor M^{1/d}\rfloor^{2}\geq\delta M^{2/d}/2
) such that
\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}\equiv\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{2,\mathbf{a}})

on
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
.

Lastly, let
\xi_{\mathrm{Taylor}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)\mapsto\mathbb{R}
be a function defined as

\xi_{\mathrm{Taylor}}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\frac{F_{0}}{\mathbf{a}!}\mathinner{\left\{2\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K})\mathinner{\left(\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x}),\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\right)}-\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\right\}}

for
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
, where
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K}\in\Theta_{(5+K,6)}^{\leq 1}
is the network parameter defined in
Lemma

N.14
. Since
\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}},\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K})\mathinner{\left(\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x}),\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\right)}-h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{mult},K})\mathinner{\left(\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x}),\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\right)}-h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt+\mathinner{\!\left\lvert h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})-h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-K}+\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\xi_{\mathrm{coef},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})-h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt+\mathinner{\!\left\lvert h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})-h_{\mathbf{a},T\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x})}(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-K}+\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\xi_{\mathrm{mon},\mathbf{a}}(\mathbf{x})-(\mathbf{x}-\mathfrak{v}(\mathbf{x}))^{\mathbf{a}}\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq(\beta^{2}+1)2^{-K}

for any
\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
and any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}
, where the second inequality follows from
Lemma

N.14
, the fourth one from
N.6
and the last one from
N.5
. Thus, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)}\mathinner{\!\left\lvert\xi_{\mathrm{Taylor}}(\mathbf{x})-P_{f}(\mathbf{x})\right\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{d,\beta}}\frac{F_{0}}{\mathbf{a}!}(3\beta^{2}+2)2^{-K}

\displaystyle\leq(3\beta^{2}+2)F_{0}(d+1)^{\lceil\beta\rceil}2^{-K}.

Combining the above display and
N.3
, we obtain the error bound in the conclusion. On the other hand, using
Lemma

N.6
again, we can show that there is a network parameter
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta_{(K^{\dagger},M^{\dagger})}^{B^{\dagger}}
with
K^{\dagger}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(K_{1}\vee K_{2})+K+6=K_{1}+K+6
,
M^{\dagger}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=M_{1}+M_{2}
and
B^{\dagger}=\max\{M+2,2(\delta M^{2/d})^{-1},F_{0}\}
such that
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta})\equiv\xi_{\mathrm{Taylor}}
on
{{\boxplus}}(T,\delta)
, which completes the proof.

N.2
Approximation of composition structured functions

N.2.1
Proof of
Theorem

K.3

We need the following technical lemma.

Lemma N.23
(Lemma 3 of
Schmidt-Hieber 2020
).

For any two functions
f^{[1]}=f_{r}^{[1]}\circ(f_{r-1,j}^{[1]})_{j\in[d_{r}]}\cdots\circ(f_{1,j}^{[1]})_{j\in[d_{2}]}
and
f^{[2]}=f_{r}^{[2]}\circ(f_{r-1,j}^{[2]})_{j\in[d_{r}]}\cdots\circ(f_{1,j}^{[2]})_{j\in[d_{2}]}
in
\mathcal{F}^{\textsc{comp}}\mathinner{\left(r,\mathbf{d},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{s},F_{0}\right)}
,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert f^{[1]}-f^{[2]}\bigr\rVert}_{\infty}\leq F_{0}(2F_{0})^{\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}\beta_{\ell}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}\max_{j\in[d_{\ell+1}]}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert f_{\ell,j}^{[1]}-f_{\ell,j}^{[2]}\bigr\rVert}_{\infty}^{\prod_{h=\ell+1}^{r}(\beta_{h}\wedge 1)}

with the convention
\prod_{h=r+1}^{r}(\beta_{h}\wedge 1)=1
.

We prove
Theorem

K.3
using
Theorems

K.1
and
N.23
as follows.

Proof N.24
(Proof of
Theorem

K.3
).

For
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}^{\textsc{comp}}\mathinner{\left(r,\mathbf{d},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{s},F_{0}\right)}
, we write
f^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=f_{r}^{\star}\circ(f_{r-1,j}^{\star})_{j\in[d_{r}]}\circ\cdots\circ(f_{1,j}^{\star})_{j\in[d_{2}]}
, where each
f_{\ell,j}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta_{\ell},s_{\ell},F_{0}}
. For notational convenience, we write
f_{r,1}^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=f_{r}^{\star}
and
d_{r+1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=1
so that the set of all component functions of
f^{\star}
can be written as
\{f_{\ell,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}j\in[d_{\ell+1}],\ell\in[r]\}
. By
Theorem

K.1
, for any large enough
\widetilde{K}\in\mathbbm{N}
and
\widetilde{M}\in\mathbbm{N}
and any
B>\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}\max_{\ell\in[r]}\widetilde{M}^{\max\{1,2(\beta_{\ell}-1)/s_{\ell}\}}
, there exists absolute constants
\mathfrak{c}_{1}^{\prime}>0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}>0
such that there is a network parameter
\bm{\theta}_{\ell,j}^{*}\in\Theta^{\leq B}_{(\widetilde{K},\widetilde{M})}
satisfying

\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell,j}^{*})-f_{\ell,j}^{\star}\bigr\rVert}_{\infty}\leq\mathfrak{c}_{2}^{\prime}(2^{-\widetilde{K}}+\widetilde{M}^{-2\beta_{\ell}/s_{\ell}})

for each
j\in[d_{\ell+1}]
and
\ell\in[r]
. Then the function defined as

\displaystyle f_{\text{net,comp}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{r,1}^{*})\circ\mathinner{\bigl(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{r-1,j}^{*})\bigr)}_{j\in[d_{r}]}\circ\cdots\circ\mathinner{\bigl(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{1,j}^{*})\bigr)}_{j\in[d_{2}]}

satisfies, by
Lemma

N.23
,

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f^{\star}-f_{\text{net,comp}}\right\rVert}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime}\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}\mathinner{\left(2^{-\widetilde{K}}+\widetilde{M}^{-2(\beta_{\ell}/s_{\ell})}\right)}^{\prod_{h=\ell+1}^{r}(\beta_{h}\wedge 1)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime}\sum_{\ell=1}^{r}\mathinner{\left(2^{-\widetilde{K}\prod_{h=\ell+1}^{r}(\beta_{h}\wedge 1)}+\widetilde{M}^{-2(\beta_{\ell}/s_{\ell})\prod_{h=\ell+1}^{r}(\beta_{h}\wedge 1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq r\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime}\mathinner{\left(\textup{e}^{-\mathfrak{c}_{4}^{\prime}\widetilde{K}}+\widetilde{M}^{-2\max_{\ell\in[r]}(\beta_{\geq\ell}/s_{\ell})}\right)}

for some absolute constants
\mathfrak{c}_{3}^{\prime}>0
and
\mathfrak{c}_{4}^{\prime}>0
, where we use the inequality
(x+y)^{u}\leq x^{u}+y^{u}
for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
u\in(0,1]
for deriving the second inequality. Furthermore, by parallelizing the neural networks
(\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}_{\ell,j}^{*}))_{j\in[d_{\ell+1}]}
for
\ell\in[r]
and staking these
r
parallelized neural networks by
Lemma

N.6
, we have the network parameter
\bm{\theta}^{*}
with depth
K\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=r\widetilde{K}
, width
M\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=2\widetilde{M}\max_{2\leq\ell\leq r-1}d_{\ell}
and magnitude bound
B
such that
\textup{{net}}(\bm{\theta}^{*})\equiv f_{\text{net,comp}}
, which completes the proof.
